\dfrac{\eta^{2}}{16}\int\limits_{\text{\hbox to0.0pt{\hss${\hphantom{\tau_{1}\rho_{1}\beta_{1}\sigma_{1}^{1}\sigma_{1}^{2}\sigma_{1}^{\omega}\tau_{2}\rho_{2}\beta_{2}\sigma_{2}^{1}\sigma_{2}^{2}\sigma_{2}^{\omega}\tau_{12}\sigma_{12}^{1}\sigma_{12}^{2}\sigma_{12}^{\omega}a_{1}a_{2}\beta_{12}}\tau_{1}\rho_{1}\beta_{1}\sigma_{1}^{1}\sigma_{1}^{2}\sigma_{1}^{\omega}\tau_{2}\rho_{2}\beta_{2}\sigma_{2}^{1}\sigma_{2}^{2}\sigma_{2}^{\omega}\tau_{12}\sigma_{12}^{1}\sigma_{12}^{2}\sigma_{12}^{\omega}a_{1}a_{2}\beta_{12}}$\hss}}}\mu^{\prime}(\beta_{12})l(\tau_{1})\mathbb{D}(1-\tau_{2})l(\tau_{12})P(-\overrightarrow{\sigma_{1}}\mathchar 59\relax\sigma_{1}^{1}\mathchar 59\relax\sigma_{1}^{2}\mathchar 59\relax\sigma_{1}^{\omega}\mathchar 59\relax\overrightarrow{\sigma_{1}})\mathbb{D}(\sigma_{1}^{1}+\overrightarrow{\sigma_{1}})\mathbb{D}(\sigma_{1}^{2})\mathbb{D}(\sigma_{2}^{1})\mathbb{D}(\sigma_{2}^{2})\mathbb{D}(\sigma_{2}^{\omega})\times\\
\times\mathbb{D}(\sigma_{12}^{\omega})\mathbb{D}(\sigma_{12}^{1})\mathbb{D}(\sigma_{12}^{2})\big[-{\mu}(\rho_{1}\mathchar 59\relax\beta_{1})\mathrm{d}{\Omega}_{1}^{2}{\mathcal{E}}+\mathbb{D}(\rho_{1}+\sigma_{1}^{1}){\mu}(\beta_{1})\mathrm{d}{\Omega}_{1}^{2}{\mathcal{E}}-2i(1-\beta_{1})\mathbb{D}(\rho_{1}+\sigma_{1}^{1}){\mu}(\beta_{1})\mathrm{d}\tau_{1}\mathrm{d}{\mathcal{E}}\big]\times\\
\times{\mu}(\rho_{2}\mathchar 59\relax\beta_{2})\mathbb{D}(a_{2}-\overrightarrow{\sigma_{2}})\mathbb{D}(a_{1}-\overrightarrow{\sigma_{1}})\mathrm{d}{\Omega}_{2}^{2}C(r_{1}\mathchar 59\relax\bar{y}\mathchar 59\relax K)\bar{*}C(r_{2}\mathchar 59\relax\bar{y}\mathchar 59\relax K)\bar{*}\omega(r_{\omega}\mathchar 59\relax\bar{y}\mathchar 59\relax K)\big|_{r_{i}=0}.

(3.56)

Expansions of
\mathrm{d}\Omega_{1}^{2}
in the first and the second terms have the form

2\mathrm{d}\tau_{1}(\Omega_{12}-\beta_{1}v_{1}-(1-\beta_{1})p_{1}+(1-\beta_{1})s)_{\alpha}p_{\omega}^{\alpha}\mathrm{d}\sigma_{1}^{\omega}(1-\tau_{1})\mathchar 59\relax

(3.57)

2\mathrm{d}\tau_{1}(\Omega_{12}-\beta_{1}v_{1}-(1-\beta_{1})p_{1}+(1-\beta_{1})s-(1-\beta_{1})(y+s+p_{+}+u_{1}))_{\alpha}p_{\omega}^{\alpha}\mathrm{d}\sigma_{1}^{\omega}(1-\tau_{1}).

(3.58)

Their difference is
2\mathrm{d}\tau_{1}(1-\beta_{1})(y+s+p_{+}+u_{1})_{\alpha}p_{\omega}^{\alpha}\mathrm{d}\sigma_{1}^{\omega}(1-\tau_{1})
. It is compensated by the third term. Thus, (
3.56
) vanishes.

Let us show only two of weak terms ignored in (
3.49
).

\begin{gathered}\dfrac{i\eta^{2}}{8}\int\limits_{\text{\hbox to0.0pt{\hss${\hphantom{\tau_{1}\rho_{1}\beta_{1}\sigma_{1}^{1}\sigma_{1}^{2}\sigma_{1}^{\omega}\tau_{2}\rho_{2}\beta_{2}\sigma_{2}^{1}\sigma_{2}^{2}\sigma_{2}^{\omega}\tau_{12}\sigma_{12}^{1}\sigma_{12}^{2}\sigma_{12}^{\omega}a_{1}a_{2}\beta_{12}}\tau_{1}\rho_{1}\beta_{1}\sigma_{1}^{1}\sigma_{1}^{2}\sigma_{1}^{\omega}\tau_{2}\rho_{2}\beta_{2}\sigma_{2}^{1}\sigma_{2}^{2}\sigma_{2}^{\omega}\tau_{12}\sigma_{12}^{1}\sigma_{12}^{2}\sigma_{12}^{\omega}a_{1}a_{2}\beta_{12}}$\hss}}}\mu^{\prime}(\beta_{12}){\mu}(\rho_{2}\mathchar 59\relax\beta_{2})\mathrm{d}{\Omega}_{2}^{2}{\mathcal{E}}C(r_{1}\mathchar 59\relax\bar{y}\mathchar 59\relax K)\bar{*}C(r_{2}\mathchar 59\relax\bar{y}\mathchar 59\relax K)\bar{*}\omega(r_{\omega}\mathchar 59\relax\bar{y}\mathchar 59\relax K)\big|_{r_{i}=0}\mathbb{D}(a_{2}-\overrightarrow{\sigma_{2}})\mathbb{D}(a_{1}-\overrightarrow{\sigma_{1}})\times\\
\times\Bigg[\bm{\mathbb{D}(\tau_{12})}P(-\overrightarrow{\sigma_{12}}\mathord{\mathchar 59\relax}\sigma_{12}^{\omega}\mathord{\mathchar 59\relax}\sigma_{12}^{2})\mathbb{D}(\sigma_{12}^{1})\mathbb{D}(\sigma_{12}^{2})\mathbb{D}(\sigma_{2}^{1}-\overrightarrow{\sigma_{2}})\mathbb{D}(\sigma_{2}^{2})l(\tau_{1})\big\{l(\tau_{2})\mathbb{D}(\sigma_{1}^{1})\mathbb{D}(\sigma_{1}^{2}-\overrightarrow{\sigma_{1}})\mathbb{D}(\sigma_{1}^{\omega}-\overrightarrow{\sigma_{1}})\times\\
\times P(-\overrightarrow{\sigma_{2}}\mathord{\mathchar 59\relax}\sigma_{2}^{\omega}\mathord{\mathchar 59\relax}\sigma_{2}^{2})\left[\mathbb{D}(\sigma_{2}^{\omega}+\overrightarrow{\sigma_{2}})-\mathbb{D}(\sigma_{12}^{\omega})\right]\mu(\rho_{1}\mathchar 59\relax\beta_{1})\mathrm{d}\Omega_{1}^{2}-\mathbb{D}(1-\tau_{2})P(-\overrightarrow{\sigma_{1}}\mathchar 59\relax\sigma_{1}^{1}\mathchar 59\relax\sigma_{1}^{2}\mathchar 59\relax\sigma_{1}^{\omega}\mathchar 59\relax\overrightarrow{\sigma_{1}})\mathbb{D}(\sigma_{1}^{2}-\sigma_{1}^{1}-\overrightarrow{\sigma_{1}})\times\\
\times\mathbb{D}(\sigma_{2}^{\omega})\left[\mathbb{D}(\sigma_{1}^{\omega}-\overrightarrow{\sigma_{1}})+\mathbb{D}(\sigma_{12}^{\omega})\right]\mathbb{D}(\rho_{1}+\sigma_{1}^{1})\mu(\beta_{1})(\mathrm{d}\Omega_{1}^{2}+2i(1-\beta_{1})\mathbb{D}(\sigma_{1}^{2})\mathrm{d}\tau_{1})\big\}\end{gathered}

(3.59)

\begin{gathered}-\bm{\mathbb{D}(1-\tau_{12})}P(-\overrightarrow{\sigma_{12}}\mathord{\mathchar 59\relax}\sigma_{12}^{\omega}\mathord{\mathchar 59\relax}\sigma_{12}^{2})\mathbb{D}(\sigma_{12}^{1})\mathbb{D}(\sigma_{12}^{2})\mathbb{D}(\sigma_{2}^{1}-\overrightarrow{\sigma_{2}})\mathbb{D}(\sigma_{2}^{2})l(\tau_{1})\mathbb{D}(1-\tau_{2})P(-\overrightarrow{\sigma_{1}}\mathchar 59\relax\sigma_{1}^{1}\mathchar 59\relax\sigma_{1}^{2}\mathchar 59\relax\sigma_{1}^{\omega}\mathchar 59\relax\overrightarrow{\sigma_{1}})\times\\
\times\mathbb{D}(\sigma_{1}^{2}-\sigma_{1}^{1}-\overrightarrow{\sigma_{1}})\mathbb{D}(\sigma_{2}^{\omega})\mathbb{D}(\sigma_{12}^{\omega})\mathbb{D}(\rho_{1}+\sigma_{1}^{1})\mu(\beta_{1})(\mathrm{d}\Omega_{1}^{2}+2i(1-\beta_{1})\mathbb{D}(\sigma_{1}^{2})\mathrm{d}\tau_{1})\Bigg].\end{gathered}

(3.60)

(
3.59
) is weak, as the form is integrated over a space whose dimension is smaller than the form degree (
\mathrm{d}z_{\alpha}=\theta_{\alpha}
, which contributes via
\Omega_{12}
, is absent).
The expression (
3.60
) is weak because the form is integrated over a space whose dimension is greater than its degree.
Other discarded weak terms can be analized analogously.

The resulting
W_{2}
again respects the property necessary for spin-locality, discussed after (
3.37
).

To find
W_{2}
in other sectors one has to consider
\mathrm{d}_{x}S_{2}+\omega*S_{2}+S_{2}*\omega
and to integrate the measure of
\sigma_{i}^{k}
. In the
{C\omega C}
sector we obtain

(\mathbb{D}(\sigma_{1}^{\omega}-\sigma_{1}^{1})\mathbb{D}(\sigma_{2}^{\omega}-\sigma_{2}^{1})-\mathbb{D}(\sigma_{1}^{\omega}-\sigma_{1}^{2})\mathbb{D}(\sigma_{2}^{\omega}-\sigma_{2}^{2}))\mathbb{D}(\sigma_{12}^{\omega})=\\
=\mathrm{d}P(\sigma_{1}^{1}\mathchar 59\relax\sigma_{1}^{\omega}\mathchar 59\relax\sigma_{1}^{2})P(\sigma_{2}^{2}\mathord{\mathchar 59\relax}\sigma_{2}^{\omega}\mathord{\mathchar 59\relax}\sigma_{2}^{1})\left[\mathbb{D}(\sigma_{2}^{\omega}-\sigma_{2}^{1})+\mathbb{D}(\sigma_{1}^{\omega}-\sigma_{1}^{2})\right]\mathbb{D}(\sigma_{12}^{\omega})\mathchar 59\relax

(3.61)

that leads to the first term in the following expression:

\begin{gathered}W_{2}|_{C\omega C}=-\dfrac{\eta^{2}}{16}\int\limits_{\text{\hbox to0.0pt{\hss${\hphantom{\tau_{1}\rho_{1}\beta_{1}\sigma_{1}^{1}\sigma_{1}^{2}\sigma_{1}^{\omega}\tau_{2}\rho_{2}\beta_{2}\sigma_{2}^{1}\sigma_{2}^{2}\sigma_{2}^{\omega}\tau_{12}\sigma_{12}^{1}\sigma_{12}^{2}\sigma_{12}^{\omega}a_{1}a_{2}\beta_{12}}\tau_{1}\rho_{1}\beta_{1}\sigma_{1}^{1}\sigma_{1}^{2}\sigma_{1}^{\omega}\tau_{2}\rho_{2}\beta_{2}\sigma_{2}^{1}\sigma_{2}^{2}\sigma_{2}^{\omega}\tau_{12}\sigma_{12}^{1}\sigma_{12}^{2}\sigma_{12}^{\omega}a_{1}a_{2}\beta_{12}}$\hss}}}\mu^{\prime}(\beta_{12})\mathbb{D}(a_{2}-\overrightarrow{\sigma_{2}})\mathbb{D}(a_{1}-\overrightarrow{\sigma_{1}})l(\tau_{12})l(\tau_{1})l(\tau_{2})P(\sigma_{1}^{1}\mathchar 59\relax\sigma_{1}^{\omega}\mathchar 59\relax\sigma_{1}^{2})\mathbb{D}(\sigma_{1}^{1})\mathbb{D}(\sigma_{1}^{2}-\overrightarrow{\sigma_{1}})\times\\
\times\mathbb{D}(\sigma_{2}^{1}-\overrightarrow{\sigma_{2}})\mathbb{D}(\sigma_{2}^{2})P(\sigma_{2}^{2}\mathord{\mathchar 59\relax}\sigma_{2}^{\omega}\mathord{\mathchar 59\relax}\sigma_{2}^{1})\left[\mathbb{D}(\sigma_{2}^{\omega}-\sigma_{2}^{1})+\mathbb{D}(\sigma_{1}^{\omega}-\sigma_{1}^{2})\right]\mathbb{D}(\sigma_{12}^{1})\mathbb{D}(\sigma_{12}^{2})\mathbb{D}(\sigma_{12}^{\omega})\times\\
\times{\mu}(\rho_{1}\mathchar 59\relax\beta_{1}){\mu}(\rho_{2}\mathchar 59\relax\beta_{2})\mathrm{d}{\Omega}_{1}^{2}\mathrm{d}{\Omega}_{2}^{2}{\mathcal{E}}C(r_{1}\mathchar 59\relax\bar{y}\mathchar 59\relax K)\bar{*}\omega(r_{\omega}\mathchar 59\relax\bar{y}\mathchar 59\relax K)\bar{*}C(r_{2}\mathchar 59\relax\bar{y}\mathchar 59\relax K)\big|_{r_{i}=0}+\\
+\dfrac{\eta^{2}}{16}\int\limits_{\text{\hbox to0.0pt{\hss${\hphantom{\tau_{1}\rho_{1}\beta_{1}\sigma_{1}^{1}\sigma_{1}^{2}\sigma_{1}^{\omega}\tau_{2}\rho_{2}\beta_{2}\sigma_{2}^{1}\sigma_{2}^{2}\sigma_{2}^{\omega}\tau_{12}\sigma_{12}^{1}\sigma_{12}^{2}\sigma_{12}^{\omega}a_{1}a_{2}\beta_{12}}\tau_{1}\rho_{1}\beta_{1}\sigma_{1}^{1}\sigma_{1}^{2}\sigma_{1}^{\omega}\tau_{2}\rho_{2}\beta_{2}\sigma_{2}^{1}\sigma_{2}^{2}\sigma_{2}^{\omega}\tau_{12}\sigma_{12}^{1}\sigma_{12}^{2}\sigma_{12}^{\omega}a_{1}a_{2}\beta_{12}}$\hss}}}\mu^{\prime}(\beta_{12})\mathbb{D}(\overrightarrow{\sigma_{1}}-a_{1})\mathbb{D}(a_{2})\mathbb{D}(\tau_{1})l(\tau_{2})l(\tau_{12})P(-\overrightarrow{\sigma_{1}}\mathchar 59\relax\sigma_{1}^{1}\mathchar 59\relax\sigma_{1}^{\omega}\mathchar 59\relax\sigma_{1}^{2}\mathchar 59\relax\overrightarrow{\sigma_{1}})\mathbb{D}(\sigma_{1}^{2}-\sigma_{1}^{1}-\overrightarrow{\sigma_{1}})\times\\
\times\mathbb{D}(\sigma_{2}^{1})\mathbb{D}(\sigma_{2}^{2})\mathbb{D}(\sigma_{2}^{\omega})\mathbb{D}(\sigma_{12}^{1})\mathbb{D}(\sigma_{12}^{2})\mathbb{D}(\sigma_{12}^{\omega})\mathbb{D}(\rho_{1}+\sigma_{1}^{1}){\mu}(\beta_{1}){\mu}(\rho_{2}\mathchar 59\relax\beta_{2})\times\\
\times\mathrm{d}{\Omega}_{1}^{2}\mathrm{d}{\Omega}_{2}^{2}{\mathcal{E}}C(r_{1}\mathchar 59\relax\bar{y}\mathchar 59\relax K)\bar{*}\omega(r_{\omega}\mathchar 59\relax\bar{y}\mathchar 59\relax K)\bar{*}C(r_{2}\mathchar 59\relax\bar{y}\mathchar 59\relax K)\big|_{r_{i}=0}\mathchar 59\relax\end{gathered}

(3.62)

where the last term can be obtain as
\vartriangleright_{\lambda}\mathrm{d}_{x}S_{1}|_{C\omega C}
, with
\lambda=\mu^{\prime}(\beta_{12})\mathbb{D}(\sigma_{12}^{1})\mathbb{D}(\sigma_{12}^{\omega})\mathbb{D}(\sigma_{12}^{2})
, and is weak.

The
{\omega CC}
sector yields

(\mathbb{D}(\sigma_{1}^{\omega}+\overrightarrow{\sigma_{1}})\mathbb{D}(\sigma_{12}^{\omega}+\overrightarrow{\sigma_{12}})+\mathbb{D}(\sigma_{1}^{\omega}-\sigma_{1}^{1})\mathbb{D}(\sigma_{12}^{\omega}-\sigma_{12}^{1}))\mathbb{D}(\sigma_{2}^{\omega}-\overrightarrow{\sigma_{2}})=\\
=-\mathrm{d}P(-\overrightarrow{\sigma_{12}}\mathord{\mathchar 59\relax}\sigma_{12}^{\omega}\mathchar 59\relax\sigma_{12}^{1})P(-\overrightarrow{\sigma_{1}}\mathord{\mathchar 59\relax}\sigma_{1}^{\omega}\mathord{\mathchar 59\relax}\sigma_{1}^{1})\left[\mathbb{D}(\sigma_{1}^{\omega}+\overrightarrow{\sigma_{1}})-\mathbb{D}(\sigma_{12}^{\omega}-\sigma_{12}^{1})\right]\mathbb{D}(\sigma_{2}^{\omega}-\overrightarrow{\sigma_{2}})\mathchar 59\relax

(3.63)

and we can obtain

\begin{gathered}W_{2}|_{\omega CC}=\dfrac{\eta^{2}}{16}\int\limits_{\text{\hbox to0.0pt{\hss${\hphantom{\tau_{1}\rho_{1}\beta_{1}\sigma_{1}^{1}\sigma_{1}^{2}\sigma_{1}^{\omega}\tau_{2}\rho_{2}\beta_{2}\sigma_{2}^{1}\sigma_{2}^{2}\sigma_{2}^{\omega}\tau_{12}\sigma_{12}^{1}\sigma_{12}^{2}\sigma_{12}^{\omega}a_{1}a_{2}\beta_{12}}\tau_{1}\rho_{1}\beta_{1}\sigma_{1}^{1}\sigma_{1}^{2}\sigma_{1}^{\omega}\tau_{2}\rho_{2}\beta_{2}\sigma_{2}^{1}\sigma_{2}^{2}\sigma_{2}^{\omega}\tau_{12}\sigma_{12}^{1}\sigma_{12}^{2}\sigma_{12}^{\omega}a_{1}a_{2}\beta_{12}}$\hss}}}\mu^{\prime}(\beta_{12})\mathbb{D}(a_{2}-\overrightarrow{\sigma_{2}})\mathbb{D}(a_{1}-\overrightarrow{\sigma_{1}})l(\tau_{12})l(\tau_{1})P(-\overrightarrow{\sigma_{12}}\mathord{\mathchar 59\relax}\sigma_{12}^{\omega}\mathchar 59\relax\sigma_{12}^{1})\mathbb{D}(\sigma_{12}^{1})\mathbb{D}(\sigma_{12}^{2})\mathbb{D}(\sigma_{2}^{1}-\overrightarrow{\sigma_{2}})\mathbb{D}(\sigma_{2}^{2})\times\\

+\dfrac{\eta^{2}}{16}\int\limits_{\text{\hbox to0.0pt{\hss${\hphantom{\tau_{1}\rho_{1}\beta_{1}\sigma_{1}^{1}\sigma_{1}^{2}\sigma_{1}^{\omega}\tau_{2}\rho_{2}\beta_{2}\sigma_{2}^{1}\sigma_{2}^{2}\sigma_{2}^{\omega}\tau_{12}\sigma_{12}^{1}\sigma_{12}^{2}\sigma_{12}^{\omega}a_{1}a_{2}\beta_{12}}\tau_{1}\rho_{1}\beta_{1}\sigma_{1}^{1}\sigma_{1}^{2}\sigma_{1}^{\omega}\tau_{2}\rho_{2}\beta_{2}\sigma_{2}^{1}\sigma_{2}^{2}\sigma_{2}^{\omega}\tau_{12}\sigma_{12}^{1}\sigma_{12}^{2}\sigma_{12}^{\omega}a_{1}a_{2}\beta_{12}}$\hss}}}\mu^{\prime}(\beta_{12})\mathbb{D}(\overrightarrow{\sigma_{1}}-a_{1})\mathbb{D}(a_{2})\mathbb{D}(\tau_{1})l(\tau_{2})l(\tau_{12})P(-\overrightarrow{\sigma_{1}}\mathchar 59\relax\sigma_{1}^{\omega}\mathchar 59\relax\sigma_{1}^{1}\mathchar 59\relax\sigma_{1}^{2}\mathchar 59\relax\overrightarrow{\sigma_{1}})\mathbb{D}(\sigma_{1}^{2}-\sigma_{1}^{1}-\overrightarrow{\sigma_{1}})\times\\
\times\mathbb{D}(\sigma_{2}^{1})\mathbb{D}(\sigma_{2}^{2})\mathbb{D}(\sigma_{2}^{\omega})\mathbb{D}(\sigma_{12}^{1})\mathbb{D}(\sigma_{12}^{2})\mathbb{D}(\sigma_{12}^{\omega})\mathbb{D}(\rho_{1}+\sigma_{1}^{1}){\mu}(\beta_{1}){\mu}(\rho_{2}\mathchar 59\relax\beta_{2})\times\\
\times\mathrm{d}{\Omega}_{1}^{2}\mathrm{d}{\Omega}_{2}^{2}{\mathcal{E}}\omega(r_{\omega}\mathchar 59\relax\bar{y}\mathchar 59\relax K)\bar{*}C(r_{1}\mathchar 59\relax\bar{y}\mathchar 59\relax K)\bar{*}C(r_{2}\mathchar 59\relax\bar{y}\mathchar 59\relax K)\big|_{r_{i}=0}\mathchar 59\relax\end{gathered}

(3.64)

where the last term is weak again.

Modulo weak terms,
W_{2}
can be represented in the following concise form:

W_{2}=\frac{1}{2i}\int\limits_{\text{\hbox to0.0pt{\hss${\hphantom{\sigma_{1}^{\omega}\sigma_{2}^{\omega}\sigma_{12}^{\omega}}\sigma_{1}^{\omega}\sigma_{2}^{\omega}\sigma_{12}^{\omega}}$\hss}}}\Big\{P(-\overrightarrow{\sigma_{12}}\mathord{\mathchar 59\relax}\sigma_{12}^{\omega}\mathord{\mathchar 59\relax}\sigma_{12}^{2})P(-\overrightarrow{\sigma_{2}}\mathord{\mathchar 59\relax}\sigma_{2}^{\omega}\mathord{\mathchar 59\relax}\sigma_{2}^{2})\mathbb{D}(\sigma_{1}^{\omega}-\overrightarrow{\sigma_{1}})\left[\mathbb{D}(\sigma_{2}^{\omega}+\overrightarrow{\sigma_{2}})-\mathbb{D}(\sigma_{12}^{\omega}-\sigma_{12}^{2})\right]S_{2}(CC\omega)+\\
-P(\sigma_{1}^{1}\mathchar 59\relax\sigma_{1}^{\omega}\mathchar 59\relax\sigma_{1}^{2})P(\sigma_{2}^{2}\mathord{\mathchar 59\relax}\sigma_{2}^{\omega}\mathord{\mathchar 59\relax}\sigma_{2}^{1})\left[\mathbb{D}(\sigma_{2}^{\omega}-\sigma_{2}^{1})+\mathbb{D}(\sigma_{1}^{\omega}-\sigma_{1}^{2})\right]\mathbb{D}(\sigma_{12}^{\omega})S_{2}(C\omega C)+\\
+P(-\overrightarrow{\sigma_{12}}\mathord{\mathchar 59\relax}\sigma_{12}^{\omega}\mathchar 59\relax\sigma_{12}^{1})P(-\overrightarrow{\sigma_{1}}\mathord{\mathchar 59\relax}\sigma_{1}^{\omega}\mathord{\mathchar 59\relax}\sigma_{1}^{1})\mathbb{D}(\sigma_{2}^{\omega}-\overrightarrow{\sigma_{2}})\left[\mathbb{D}(\sigma_{1}^{\omega}+\overrightarrow{\sigma_{1}})-\mathbb{D}(\sigma_{12}^{\omega}-\sigma_{12}^{1})\right]S_{2}(\omega CC)\Big\}.

(3.65)

Here
S_{2}(\omega CC)
denotes the expression for
S_{2}
(
3.35
) with
C\bar{*}C
replaced by
\omega\bar{*}C\bar{*}C
and analogously for
S_{2}(C\omega C)
and
S_{2}(CC\omega)
. Analogous remarkable representation for
W_{1}
via
S_{1}
was found in
[
34
]
. However, as argued in Appendix
B.2.2
, such relations are not anticipated to work in higher orders.

3.7
Field equation on the physical field

In the second order, the equation on the one-form field has the form

\mathrm{d}_{x}W_{1}+\mathrm{d}_{x}W_{2}+\omega*W_{2}+W_{2}*\omega+W_{1}*W_{1}-2i\mathrm{d}W_{2}^{(2)}\cong 0\,.

(3.66)

Here
\mathrm{d}_{x}W_{1}
contributes as a result of the action of
\mathrm{d}_{x}
on
C
and
\omega
in
W_{1}
.

Let us consider the
CC\omega\omega
sector. The following two terms come from
\mathrm{d}_{x}W_{1}
:

\mathrm{d}_{x}W_{1}|_{CC\omega\omega}=W_{1}(C\mathchar 59\relax\mathrm{d}_{x}\omega)|_{CC\omega\omega}+W_{1}(\mathrm{d}_{x}C\mathchar 59\relax\omega)|_{CC\omega\omega}\,.

(3.67)

The first one has the form

W_{1}(C\mathrm{d}_{x}\omega)|_{CC\omega\omega}=\dfrac{i\eta}{4}\int\limits_{\text{\hbox to0.0pt{\hss${\hphantom{\tau\rho\beta\sigma\sigma^{\omega}}\tau\rho\beta\sigma\sigma^{\omega}}$\hss}}}l(\tau)\mathbb{D}(\sigma)P(\sigma\mathchar 59\relax\sigma^{\omega}\mathchar 59\relax 1-\beta)\mu(\rho\mathchar 59\relax\beta)\mathrm{d}\Omega^{2}\mathcal{E}(\Omega)C(r_{1})\bar{*}(\mathrm{d}_{x}\omega(r_{\omega}))|_{C\omega\omega}\big|_{r=0}k\,.

(3.68)

Here
\mathrm{d}_{x}\omega
has to be substituted by the contribution to the sector

C\omega\omega
from the
r.h.s.
of (
3.20
). Then (
3.68
)
is transformed to the form (
2.40
) analogously to how it has been proceeded with (
3.43
). Let the objects arising from
W_{1}
and
\mathrm{d}_{x}{\omega}
be endowed by the labels 1 and 2, respectively. Then
we rename
p_{\alpha}^{\omega}\rightarrow s_{\alpha}
and
r_{\alpha}^{\omega}\rightarrow-t_{\alpha}
. Note that the coefficient in front of
s_{\alpha}
in the resulting
\Omega_{1}
is not constant since it arises from
\sigma^{\omega}
in (
3.68
). The exponent in the integrand takes the form

\exp i\big[\Omega_{\alpha}(y+s+p_{1}+u_{1})^{\alpha}-(p(\sigma_{2})-\beta_{2}v_{2})_{\alpha}(-t+p_{2}+p_{\omega 1}+p_{\omega 2}+u_{2})^{\alpha}+s_{\alpha}t^{\alpha}+s_{\alpha}p_{1}^{\alpha}+\\
+u_{1{\alpha}}v_{1}^{\alpha}+u_{2{\alpha}}v_{2}^{\alpha}-p_{2{\alpha}}p_{\omega 1}^{\alpha}-p_{2{\alpha}}p_{\omega 2}^{\alpha}-p_{\omega 1{\alpha}}p_{\omega 2}^{\alpha}-\sum_{i=1}^{4}p_{i{\alpha}}r_{i}^{\alpha}\big]=\exp i\big[\Omega_{\alpha}(y+s+p_{1}+u_{1})^{\alpha}+\\
-(-p(\sigma_{2})-t+\beta_{2}v_{2})_{\alpha}(t-p_{2}-p_{\omega 1}-p_{\omega 2}-u_{2})^{\alpha}+s_{\alpha}t^{\alpha}+s_{\alpha}p_{1}^{\alpha}+u_{1{\alpha}}v_{1}^{\alpha}+u_{2{\alpha}}v_{2}^{\alpha}-p_{2{\alpha}}p_{\omega 1}^{\alpha}-p_{2{\alpha}}p_{\omega 2}^{\alpha}+\\
-p_{\omega 1{\alpha}}p_{\omega 2}^{\alpha}-\sum_{i=1}^{4}p_{i{\alpha}}r_{i}^{\alpha}-t_{\alpha}(-p_{2}-p_{\omega 1}-p_{\omega 2}-u_{2})^{\alpha}\big].

(3.69)

To bring this expression to the form (
2.40
) we change the signs of
u_{2\alpha}
and
v_{2\alpha}
and then make the substitutions
s_{\alpha}\rightarrow s_{\alpha}+p_{2\alpha}+p_{\omega 1{\alpha}}+p_{\omega 2{\alpha}}
,
t_{\alpha}\rightarrow t_{\alpha}+y_{\alpha}-p_{1\alpha}
,
v_{2\alpha}\rightarrow v_{2\alpha}-t_{\alpha}+p_{1\alpha}
. This yields

\times P(\sigma_{1}^{1}\mathord{\mathchar 59\relax}\sigma_{1}^{\omega 1}\mathchar 59\relax\sigma_{1}^{\omega 2}\mathchar 59\relax\sigma_{1}^{2})P(\sigma_{2}^{2}\mathord{\mathchar 59\relax}\sigma_{2}^{\omega 2}\mathchar 59\relax\sigma_{2}^{\omega 1}\mathchar 59\relax\sigma_{2}^{1})\mathbb{D}(\sigma_{1}^{\omega 2}-\sigma_{1}^{2})\mathbb{D}(\sigma_{2}^{\omega 1}-\sigma_{2}^{1})\mathbb{D}(\sigma_{12}^{1})\mathbb{D}(\sigma_{12}^{2})\mathbb{D}(\sigma_{12}^{\omega 1})\mathbb{D}(\sigma_{12}^{\omega 2})\times\\
\times{\mu}(\rho_{1}\mathchar 59\relax\beta_{1}){\mu}(\rho_{2}\mathchar 59\relax\beta_{2})\mathrm{d}{\Omega}_{1}^{2}\mathrm{d}{\Omega}_{2}^{2}\mathcal{E}(\Omega_{1}\mathchar 59\relax\Omega_{2})C(r_{1}\mathchar 59\relax\bar{y}\mathchar 59\relax K)\bar{*}\omega(r_{\omega 1}\mathchar 59\relax\bar{y}\mathchar 59\relax K)\bar{*}\omega(r_{\omega 2}\mathchar 59\relax\bar{y}\mathchar 59\relax K)\bar{*}C(r_{2}\mathchar 59\relax\bar{y}\mathchar 59\relax K)\big|_{r_{i}=0}\,\mathchar 59\relax

(3.78)

\Upsilon(\omega CC\omega)=-\dfrac{\eta^{2}}{16}\int\limits_{\text{\hbox to0.0pt{\hss${\hskip 16.38895pt\hskip 16.38895pt\hskip 16.38895pt\hskip 16.38895pt\hskip 16.38895pt\hskip 16.38895pt\hskip 16.38895pt\hskip 16.38895pt\tau_{1}\rho_{1}\beta_{1}\sigma_{1}^{1}\sigma_{1}^{2}\sigma_{1}^{\omega 1}\sigma_{1}^{\omega 2}\tau_{2}\rho_{2}\beta_{2}\sigma_{2}^{1}\sigma_{2}^{2}\sigma_{2}^{\omega 1}\sigma_{2}^{\omega 2}\tau_{12}\sigma_{12}^{1}\sigma_{12}^{2}\sigma_{12}^{\omega 1}\sigma_{12}^{\omega 2}\beta_{12}}$\hss}}}\mu^{\prime}(\beta_{12})\mathbb{D}(\tau_{12})l(\tau_{1})P(-\overrightarrow{\sigma_{1}}\mathchar 59\relax\sigma_{1}^{\omega 1}\mathchar 59\relax\sigma_{1}^{1}\mathchar 59\relax\sigma_{1}^{2}\mathchar 59\relax\sigma_{1}^{\omega 2}\mathchar 59\relax\overrightarrow{\sigma_{1}})P(-\overrightarrow{\sigma_{2}}\mathchar 59\relax\sigma_{2}^{\omega 2}\mathchar 59\relax\sigma_{2}^{2}\mathchar 59\relax\sigma_{2}^{1}\mathchar 59\relax\sigma_{2}^{\omega 1}\mathchar 59\relax\overrightarrow{\sigma_{2}})\times\\
\times P(-\overrightarrow{\sigma_{12}}\mathchar 59\relax\sigma_{12}^{\omega 2}\mathchar 59\relax\sigma_{12}^{2})P(-\overrightarrow{\sigma_{12}}\mathchar 59\relax\sigma_{12}^{\omega 1}\mathord{\mathchar 59\relax}\sigma_{12}^{1})\mathbb{D}(\sigma_{12}^{1})\mathbb{D}(\sigma_{12}^{2})\mathbb{D}(\sigma_{1}^{\omega 2}-\overrightarrow{\sigma_{1}})\mathbb{D}(\sigma_{2}^{\omega 1}-\overrightarrow{\sigma_{2}})\mathbb{D}(\sigma_{2}^{1}-\overrightarrow{\sigma_{2}})\mathbb{D}(\sigma_{2}^{2})\times\\
\times\big\{l(\tau_{2})\mathbb{D}(\sigma_{1}^{1})\mathbb{D}(\sigma_{1}^{2}-\overrightarrow{\sigma_{1}})\big[\mathbb{D}(\sigma_{12}^{\omega 1})\mathbb{D}(\sigma_{12}^{\omega 2})+\mathbb{D}(\sigma_{2}^{\omega 2}+\overrightarrow{\sigma_{2}})\mathbb{D}(\sigma_{12}^{\omega 1})+\mathbb{D}(\sigma_{1}^{\omega 1}+\overrightarrow{\sigma_{1}})\mathbb{D}(\sigma_{12}^{\omega 2})+\\
-\mathbb{D}(\sigma_{1}^{\omega 1}+\overrightarrow{\sigma_{1}})\mathbb{D}(\sigma_{2}^{\omega 2}+\overrightarrow{\sigma_{2}})\big]{\mu}(\rho_{1}\mathchar 59\relax\beta_{1})\mathrm{d}{\Omega}_{1}^{2}+\\
-\mathbb{D}(1-\tau_{2})\mathbb{D}(\sigma_{1}^{2}-\sigma_{1}^{1}-\overrightarrow{\sigma_{1}})\mathbb{D}(\sigma_{1}^{\omega 1}+\overrightarrow{\sigma_{1}})\mathbb{D}(\sigma_{2}^{\omega 2})\mathbb{D}(\rho_{1}+\sigma_{1}^{1}){\mu}(\beta_{1})(\mathrm{d}\Omega_{1}^{2}+2i(1-\beta_{1})\mathbb{D}(\sigma_{1}^{2})\mathrm{d}\tau_{1})\big\}\times\\
\times{\mu}(\rho_{2}\mathchar 59\relax\beta_{2})\mathrm{d}{\Omega}_{2}^{2}\mathcal{E}(\Omega_{1}\mathchar 59\relax\Omega_{2})\omega(r_{\omega 1}\mathchar 59\relax\bar{y}\mathchar 59\relax K)\bar{*}C(r_{1}\mathchar 59\relax\bar{y}\mathchar 59\relax K)\bar{*}C(r_{2}\mathchar 59\relax\bar{y}\mathchar 59\relax K)\bar{*}\omega(r_{\omega 2}\mathchar 59\relax\bar{y}\mathchar 59\relax K)\big|_{r_{i}=0}\,\mathchar 59\relax

(3.79)

\Upsilon(\omega\omega CC)=-\dfrac{\eta^{2}}{16}\int\limits_{\text{\hbox to0.0pt{\hss${\hphantom{\tau_{1}\rho_{1}\beta_{1}\sigma_{1}^{1}\sigma_{1}^{2}\sigma_{1}^{\omega 1}\sigma_{1}^{\omega 2}\tau_{2}\rho_{2}\beta_{2}\sigma_{2}^{1}\sigma_{2}^{2}\sigma_{2}^{\omega 1}\sigma_{2}^{\omega 2}\tau_{12}\sigma_{12}^{1}\sigma_{12}^{2}\sigma_{12}^{\omega 1}\sigma_{12}^{\omega 2}}\tau_{1}\rho_{1}\beta_{1}\sigma_{1}^{1}\sigma_{1}^{2}\sigma_{1}^{\omega 1}\sigma_{1}^{\omega 2}\tau_{2}\rho_{2}\beta_{2}\sigma_{2}^{1}\sigma_{2}^{2}\sigma_{2}^{\omega 1}\sigma_{2}^{\omega 2}\tau_{12}\sigma_{12}^{1}\sigma_{12}^{2}\sigma_{12}^{\omega 1}\sigma_{12}^{\omega 2}}$\hss}}}\mu^{\prime}(\beta_{12})\mathbb{D}(\tau_{12})l(\tau_{1})P(-\overrightarrow{\sigma_{12}}\mathchar 59\relax\sigma_{12}^{\omega 1}\mathchar 59\relax\sigma_{12}^{\omega 2}\mathchar 59\relax\sigma_{12}^{2})P(-\overrightarrow{\sigma_{1}}\mathchar 59\relax\sigma_{1}^{\omega 1}\mathchar 59\relax\sigma_{1}^{\omega 2}\mathchar 59\relax\sigma_{1}^{1}\mathchar 59\relax\sigma_{1}^{2}\mathchar 59\relax\overrightarrow{\sigma_{1}})\mathbb{D}(\sigma_{12}^{1})\mathbb{D}(\sigma_{12}^{2})\times\\
\times\mathbb{D}(\sigma_{2}^{1}-\overrightarrow{\sigma_{2}})\mathbb{D}(\sigma_{2}^{2})\mathbb{D}(\sigma_{2}^{\omega 1}-\overrightarrow{\sigma_{2}})\mathbb{D}(\sigma_{2}^{\omega 2}-\overrightarrow{\sigma_{2}})\mathbb{D}(\sigma_{1}^{\omega 1}+\overrightarrow{\sigma_{1}})\big\{l(\tau_{2})\mathbb{D}(\sigma_{1}^{1})\mathbb{D}(\sigma_{1}^{2}-\overrightarrow{\sigma_{1}})\ \big[\mathbb{D}(\sigma_{1}^{\omega 2}+\overrightarrow{\sigma_{1}})+\mathbb{D}(\sigma_{12}^{\omega 2})\big]\times\\

(3.80)

\Upsilon(\omega C\omega C)=-\dfrac{\eta^{2}}{16}\int\limits_{\text{\hbox to0.0pt{\hss${\hphantom{\tau_{1}\rho_{1}\beta_{1}\sigma_{1}^{1}\sigma_{1}^{2}\sigma_{1}^{\omega 1}\sigma_{1}^{\omega 2}\tau_{2}\rho_{2}\beta_{2}\sigma_{2}^{1}\sigma_{2}^{2}\sigma_{2}^{\omega 1}\sigma_{2}^{\omega 2}\tau_{12}\sigma_{12}^{1}\sigma_{12}^{2}\sigma_{12}^{\omega 1}\sigma_{12}^{\omega 2}\beta_{12}}\tau_{1}\rho_{1}\beta_{1}\sigma_{1}^{1}\sigma_{1}^{2}\sigma_{1}^{\omega 1}\sigma_{1}^{\omega 2}\tau_{2}\rho_{2}\beta_{2}\sigma_{2}^{1}\sigma_{2}^{2}\sigma_{2}^{\omega 1}\sigma_{2}^{\omega 2}\tau_{12}\sigma_{12}^{1}\sigma_{12}^{2}\sigma_{12}^{\omega 1}\sigma_{12}^{\omega 2}\beta_{12}}$\hss}}}\mu^{\prime}(\beta_{12})\mathbb{D}(\tau_{12})l(\tau_{1})\big\{l(\tau_{2})\mathbb{D}(\sigma_{1}^{1})\mathbb{D}(\sigma_{1}^{2}-\overrightarrow{\sigma_{1}})\mathbb{D}(\sigma_{2}^{1}-\overrightarrow{\sigma_{2}})\mathbb{D}(\sigma_{2}^{2})\mathbb{D}(\sigma_{2}^{\omega 1}-\overrightarrow{\sigma_{2}})\times\\
\times P(-\overrightarrow{\sigma_{12}}\mathchar 59\relax\sigma_{12}^{\omega 1}\mathchar 59\relax\sigma_{12}^{1})P(-\overrightarrow{\sigma_{1}}\mathchar 59\relax\sigma_{1}^{\omega 1}\mathchar 59\relax\sigma_{1}^{1}\mathord{\mathchar 59\relax}\sigma_{1}^{\omega 2}\mathchar 59\relax\sigma_{1}^{2})P(\sigma_{2}^{2}\mathchar 59\relax\sigma_{2}^{\omega 2}\mathchar 59\relax\sigma_{2}^{1}\mathchar 59\relax\sigma_{2}^{\omega 1}\mathchar 59\relax\overrightarrow{\sigma_{2}})\left[\mathbb{D}(\sigma_{1}^{\omega 2}-\sigma_{1}^{2})\mathbb{D}(\sigma_{1}^{\omega 1}+\overrightarrow{\sigma_{1}})+\right.\\
\left.+\mathbb{D}(\sigma_{2}^{\omega 2}-\sigma_{2}^{1})\mathbb{D}(\sigma_{1}^{\omega 1}+\overrightarrow{\sigma_{1}})+\mathbb{D}(\sigma_{12}^{\omega 1})\mathbb{D}(\sigma_{1}^{\omega 2}-\sigma_{1}^{2})\right]\mathbb{D}(\sigma_{12}^{1})\mathbb{D}(\sigma_{12}^{2})\mathbb{D}(\sigma_{12}^{\omega 2})\times\\
\times{\mu}(\rho_{1}\mathchar 59\relax\beta_{1}){\mu}(\rho_{2}\mathchar 59\relax\beta_{2})\mathrm{d}{\Omega}_{1}^{2}\mathrm{d}{\Omega}_{2}^{2}\mathcal{E}(\Omega_{1}\mathchar 59\relax\Omega_{2})\omega(r_{\omega 1}\mathchar 59\relax\bar{y}\mathchar 59\relax K)\bar{*}C(r_{1}\mathchar 59\relax\bar{y}\mathchar 59\relax K)\bar{*}\omega(r_{\omega 2}\mathchar 59\relax\bar{y}\mathchar 59\relax K)\bar{*}C(r_{2}\mathchar 59\relax\bar{y}\mathchar 59\relax K)\big|_{r_{i}=0}\,.

(3.81)

This list exhausts all quadratic holomorphic (
i.e.,
proportional to
\eta^{2}
) vertices. Antiholomorphic vertices can be obtained from the holomorphic ones by
complex conjugation. The construction of mixed vertices involving both
\eta
and
\bar{\eta}
is an important open problem for the future.

4
Conclusion

In this paper, the differential homotopy approach of
[
34
]
,
originally developed for the lower order analysis of HS equations, is
further extended in a way appropriate for the higher order computations. We have modified the Fundamental Ansatz of
[
34
]
in a way appropriate for the next order of perturbation theory. Useful properties of this modification have been presented.
In particular, we have generalized the star-multiplication formulae of
[
34
]
and derived symmetry properties (
2.53
), (
2.62
). A particularly interesting new result is the star-multiplication formula (
2.31
) involving an
arbitrary number of Ansatzes from
[
34
]
(canonical functions (
2.12
)).

One of significant results of
[
34
]
was the appropriate homotopy procedure for finding the second-order field
B_{2pc}
, that leads to projectively compact vertices in the zero-form sector. In our paper, we provide a comparison between the differential homotopy and shifted homotopy approaches. It is shown how the shifted homotopy approach can be represented in terms of the differential one. Thus, the differential homotopy generalizes and extends the shifted one. In addition, we have shown in
Appendix C
that the difference between
B_{2pc}
and
B_{2sh}
of
[
34
]
coincides with the shift
\delta B
of
[
36
]
, that leads to a minimal number of derivatives in the HS equations.

The developed formalism has made it possible to obtain quadratic vertices in the
one-form equation of the holomorphic sector. These results match those of
[
27
]
, reproducing in particular such their properties as spinor spin-locality and projective-compactness, which in turn imply space-time spin-locality
[
24
]
.

Our results were derived with an arbitrary dependence of the measure on the parameter

\beta
playing instrumental role in taking the spin-local limit
\beta\to-\infty
. The approach developed in this paper provides a basis
for extending the obtained results to higher orders in a reasonably simple
and systematic way.
In particular, it allows one to make computations directly without using the sophisticated analysis of the type of
[
29
]
relying on numerous auxiliary theorems about properties of various classes of HS field functions.

An interesting direction for future research would be to extend the developed formalism to HS equations in the mixed sector (
i.e.
​​, neither purely holomorphic nor purely antiholomorphic) of the theory.

Appendix A
Ansatz multiplication computations

A.1
The proof by induction

Formula (
2.31
) can be proven by induction. At
J=1
it reproduces (
2.12
) because
s_{1}

and
t_{1}
are absent in that case. To proceed, consider

F_{J}*f_{J+1}=\int\limits_{\text{\hbox to0.0pt{\hss${\hphantom{pruvst\tau\rho\beta\sigma}pruvst\tau\rho\beta\sigma}$\hss}}}\delta^{2}(t_{1})\delta^{2}(s_{J+1})\mathcal{E}_{J+1}\prod_{j=1}^{J+1}\mathrm{d}^{2}u_{j}\mathrm{d}^{2}v_{j}\mathrm{d}^{2}p_{j}\mathrm{d}^{2}r_{j}\mathrm{d}^{2}s_{j}\mathrm{d}^{2}t_{j}\mu_{j}(\tau_{j}\mathchar 59\relax\rho_{j}\mathchar 59\relax\beta_{j}\mathchar 59\relax\sigma_{j})\mathrm{d}\tilde{\Omega}_{j}^{2}\\
g_{j\mathord{\mathchar 59\relax}1}(r_{j\mathord{\mathchar 59\relax}1})\overline{*}\dots\overline{*}g_{j\mathord{\mathchar 59\relax}n_{j}}(r_{j\mathord{\mathchar 59\relax}n_{j}})k^{J+1}\mathchar 59\relax

(A.1)

where

\mathcal{E}_{J+1}=\exp i\sum_{j=1}^{J}\big[(s_{j\alpha}+s_{j-1\alpha})t_{j}^{\alpha}+(s_{j\alpha}-t_{j\alpha})(q_{j}^{\alpha}+s_{J}^{\alpha}+u_{j}^{\alpha})+u_{j\alpha}v_{j}^{\alpha}+\\
+(\tilde{\Omega}_{j\alpha}+s_{J\alpha})(q_{j}^{\alpha}+s_{J}^{\alpha}+u_{j}^{\alpha}+s_{j}^{\alpha}+t_{j}^{\alpha})-p_{j\alpha}r^{\alpha}+(\tilde{\Omega}_{J+1\alpha}-t_{J+1\alpha})(q_{J+1}^{\alpha}+t_{J+1}^{\alpha}+u_{J+1}^{\alpha})+\\
+s_{J\alpha}t_{J+1}^{\alpha}+u_{J+1\alpha}v_{J+1}^{\alpha}-\sum_{i}p_{j\mathord{\mathchar 59\relax}i\alpha}r_{j\mathord{\mathchar 59\relax}i}^{\alpha}-\sum_{i<i^{\prime}}p_{j\mathord{\mathchar 59\relax}i\alpha}p_{j\mathord{\mathchar 59\relax}i^{\prime}}^{\alpha}\big]\mathchar 59\relax

(A.2)

\tilde{\Omega}_{j\alpha}=\tau_{j}z_{\alpha}-(1-\tau_{j})((-1)^{j+1}p_{j\alpha}(\sigma_{j})-\beta_{j}v_{j\alpha}+s_{j\alpha}+s_{J\alpha}-t_{j\alpha}+\rho_{j}(q_{j\alpha}+u_{j\alpha}+s_{j\alpha}+t_{j\alpha}))\\
\text{for }j\leq J\mathchar 59\relax

(A.3)

\tilde{\Omega}_{J+1\alpha}=\tau_{J+1}z_{\alpha}-(1-\tau_{J+1})((-1)^{J}p_{J+1\alpha}(\sigma_{J+1})-\beta_{J+1}v_{J+1\alpha}-t_{J+1\alpha}+\rho_{J+1}(q_{J+1\alpha}+u_{J+1\alpha}+t_{J+1\alpha})).

A.2
Reduced formula

Shifting

\mathcal{E}_{J}=\exp i\sum_{j=1}^{J}\left[(s_{j\alpha}+s_{j-1\alpha})t_{j}^{\alpha}+(s_{j\alpha}-t_{j\alpha})u_{j}^{\alpha}+(u_{j\alpha}-q_{j\alpha})v_{j}^{\alpha}+\tilde{\Omega}_{j\alpha}(u_{j}^{\alpha}+s_{j}^{\alpha}+t_{j}^{\alpha})\vphantom{\sum_{i}}\right.-\\

and

and then integrated away in the Gaussian integral. Indeed, changing the variables

+\left.c_{j-1}b_{j}u_{j-1\alpha}u_{j}^{\alpha}+(u_{j\alpha}-q_{j\alpha})v_{j}^{\alpha}+\tilde{\Omega}_{j\alpha}(s_{j}^{\alpha}+t_{j}^{\alpha})-\sum_{i}p_{j\mathord{\mathchar 59\relax}i\alpha}r_{j\mathord{\mathchar 59\relax}i}^{\alpha}-\sum_{i<i^{\prime}}p_{j\mathord{\mathchar 59\relax}i\alpha}p_{j\mathord{\mathchar 59\relax}i^{\prime}}^{\alpha}\right]\mathchar 59\relax

(A.10)

\tilde{\Omega}_{i}^{\alpha}=\tau_{i}z^{\alpha}-(1-\tau_{i})\left((-1)^{i+1}p_{i}^{\alpha}(\sigma_{i})-\beta_{i}v_{i}^{\alpha}+s_{i}^{\alpha}-t_{i}^{\alpha}-(c_{i}-b_{i})u_{i}^{\alpha}+\rho_{i}(s_{i}^{\alpha}+t_{i}^{\alpha})\right).

(A.11)

Shifting the variables
v_{j}^{\alpha}

\mathcal{E}_{J}=\exp i\sum_{j=1}^{J}\left[(s_{j\alpha}+s_{j-1\alpha})t_{j}^{\alpha}+(c_{j}(s_{j\alpha}+t_{j+1\alpha})-b_{j}(t_{j\alpha}+s_{j-1\alpha}))q_{j}^{\alpha}\vphantom{\sum_{i}}\right.+\\
+\left.c_{j-1}b_{j}u_{j-1\alpha}u_{j}^{\alpha}+(u_{j\alpha}-q_{j\alpha})v_{j}^{\alpha}+\tilde{\Omega}_{j\alpha}(s_{j}^{\alpha}+t_{j}^{\alpha})-\sum_{i}p_{j\mathord{\mathchar 59\relax}i\alpha}r_{j\mathord{\mathchar 59\relax}i}^{\alpha}-\sum_{i<i^{\prime}}p_{j\mathord{\mathchar 59\relax}i\alpha}p_{j\mathord{\mathchar 59\relax}i^{\prime}}^{\alpha}\right]\mathchar 59\relax

\tilde{\Omega}_{i}^{\alpha}=\tau_{i}z^{\alpha}-(1-\tau_{i})\left((-1)^{i+1}p_{i}^{\alpha}(\sigma_{i})-\beta_{i}(v_{i}^{\alpha}+c_{i}(s_{i}^{\alpha}+t_{i+1}^{\alpha})-b_{i}(t_{i}^{\alpha}+s_{i-1}^{\alpha}))+\right.\\
\left.+s_{i}^{\alpha}-t_{i}^{\alpha}-(c_{i}-b_{i})q_{i}^{\alpha}+\rho_{i}(s_{i}^{\alpha}+t_{i}^{\alpha})\right).

Let us now specify

As a result, integration over

(A.22)

and

v_{k+1}^{\alpha}\to v_{k+1}^{\alpha}+q_{k}^{\alpha}\mathchar 59\relax\qquad v_{k}^{\alpha}\to v_{k}^{\alpha}-q_{k+1}^{\alpha}.

(A.23)

The shift (
A.23
) compensates the
qv
-type terms. One can also see that the shift (
A.22
) compensates the
sq
- and
tq
-type terms. Note that this shift is consistent with
s_{J}^{\alpha}=t_{1}^{\alpha}=0
since we consider
c_{J}=b_{1}=0\mathchar 59\relax c_{1}=b_{J}=1
:

s_{J}^{\alpha}\to s_{J}^{\alpha}-1\cdot n_{J}^{\alpha}+n_{J}^{\alpha}=s_{J}^{\alpha}\mathchar 59\relax\qquad t_{1}^{\alpha}\to t_{1}^{\alpha}.

(A.24)

Using (
A.22
) from here it follows that

s_{i}^{\alpha}+t_{i}^{\alpha}\to s_{i}^{\alpha}+t_{i}^{\alpha}+(-1)^{i}n_{J}^{\alpha}.

(A.25)

The leftover terms bilinear in
n_{i}^{\alpha}
can be seen to acquire a simple form
-\sum_{j=1}^{J}n_{j-1\alpha}n_{j}^{\alpha}
.
To prove this, it is useful to employ equation (
A.17
) and some other identities involving
c_{i}
and
b_{i}
:

c_{i}b_{i}=0\mathchar 59\relax\qquad c_{i}+b_{i-1}=1-\delta_{i-k-1\mathchar 59\relax 0}\mathchar 59\relax

(A.26)

\quad b_{i}b_{i+i^{\prime}}=b_{i}\mathchar 59\relax\quad c_{i}c_{i-i^{\prime}}=c_{i}\quad\text{for }i^{\prime}\geq 0\,\mathchar 59\relax

(A.27)

that are valid for
c_{i}
and
b_{i}
defined as in (
2.38
). This brings (
A.20
) to the form

\mathcal{E}_{J}=\exp i\Bigg(\sum_{j=1}^{J}\left[(s_{j\alpha}+s_{j-1\alpha})t_{j}^{\alpha}+\tilde{\Omega}_{j\alpha}(s_{j}^{\alpha}+t_{j}^{\alpha})-n_{j-1\alpha}n_{j}^{\alpha}-\sum_{i}p_{j\mathord{\mathchar 59\relax}i\alpha}r_{j\mathord{\mathchar 59\relax}i}^{\alpha}-\sum_{i<i^{\prime}}p_{j\mathord{\mathchar 59\relax}i\alpha}p_{j\mathord{\mathchar 59\relax}i^{\prime}}^{\alpha}\right]-\\
-v_{k\alpha}v_{k+1}^{\alpha}\Bigg).

(A.28)

To evaluate
\sum_{j=1}^{J}n_{j-1\alpha}n_{j}^{\alpha}
we use (
A.21
)

-\sum_{j=1}^{J}n_{j-1\alpha}n_{j}^{\alpha}=\sum_{j=1}^{J}(-1)^{j}q_{j\alpha}n_{j}^{\alpha}=\sum_{J\geq j>j^{\prime}\geq 1}(-1)^{j+j^{\prime}}q_{j\alpha}q_{j^{\prime}}^{\alpha}.

(A.29)

From (
2.34
), it follows that this expression contains the terms bilinear in
p
and those linear in
y
. The former have the form
\sum_{j>j^{\prime}}p_{(j)+\alpha}p_{(j^{\prime})+}^{\alpha}
. The form of
y
-dependent terms depends on whether
J
is odd or even. The final result is

-\sum_{j=1}^{J}n_{j-1\alpha}n_{j}^{\alpha}=-\sum_{j<j^{\prime}}p_{(j)+\alpha}p_{(j^{\prime})+}^{\alpha}-|J+1|_{2}\sum_{j=1}^{J}y_{\alpha}p_{(j)+}^{\alpha}\mathchar 59\relax

(A.30)

with the convention (
2.37
).

This yields (
2.35
), (
2.36
).

Appendix B
Relation to the
\beta
-shifted contracting homotopy method

In this section, we demonstrate that shifted homotopies can be implemented as differential homotopies, thereby proving that the latter approach is more general. That the two methods are not fully equivalent follows from the construction of a differential homotopy for solving the
B_{2}
-field equation in
[
34
]
, that admits no shifted homotopy realization.

B.1
Homotopy operator

We propose the following operator acting on differential forms
F
:

\left(\vphantom{\big|}\vartriangleright_{\lambda}F(z\mathchar 59\relax y|\theta)\right)[\tau\mathchar 59\relax\sigma^{i}\mathchar 59\relax\beta\mathchar 59\relax u\mathord{\mathchar 59\relax}v]=d^{2}u\,d^{2}v\,\lambda(\sigma^{i}\mathchar 59\relax\beta)\,l(\tau)\,F(\Omega\mathchar 59\relax y+u|\mathrm{d}\Omega)\,\exp\left(iu_{\alpha}v^{\alpha}\right)\mathchar 59\relax

(B.1)

where
\Omega_{\alpha}
is given by

\Omega_{\alpha}=\tau z_{\alpha}-(1-\tau)\left[p_{\alpha}(\sigma)-\beta v_{\alpha}\right]\mathchar 59\relax

(B.2)

and
\lambda(\sigma^{i}\mathchar 59\relax\beta)
is a closed differential form.

This operator has several important characteristics:

•

It acts on differential forms depending on the variables
z_{\alpha}
,
y_{\alpha}
, and additional homotopy variables;

•

The result is a differential form in an extended space, which includes:

–

The original variables
z_{\alpha}
,
y_{\alpha}
and additional homotopy variables;

–

New variables
\tau
,
\beta
,
\sigma^{i}
;

–

Auxiliary spinor variables
u_{\alpha}
,
v_{\alpha}
.

To establish connection with known results, we consider a specific form of the measure
\lambda
. In particular, choosing

\lambda(\sigma^{i}\mathchar 59\relax\beta)=\lambda_{0}(\sigma^{i}\mathchar 59\relax\beta)=\prod_{i}\mathbb{D}(\sigma^{i}-\sigma_{0}^{i})\cdot\mathbb{D}(\beta-\beta_{0})\mathchar 59\relax

(B.3)

where
\sigma_{0}^{i}
and
\beta_{0}
are constant parameters, reproduces in a weak sense the standard results obtained through the
\beta
-shifted contracting homotopy technique

\Delta_{p(\sigma_{0})\mathchar 59\relax\beta_{0}}F=\int\limits_{\text{\hbox to0.0pt{\hss${\hphantom{u^{2}v^{2}}u^{2}v^{2}}$\hss}}}d^{2}ud^{2}v\int\limits_{\text{\hbox to0.0pt{\hss${\hphantom{\tau}\tau}$\hss}}}\dfrac{d\tau}{\tau}l(\tau)(z+p(\sigma_{0})-\beta_{0}v)^{\alpha}\dfrac{1}{\partial\theta^{\alpha}}F(\Omega_{0}\mathchar 59\relax y+u|\tau\theta)\exp iu_{\gamma}v^{\gamma}\mathchar 59\relax

(B.4)

with
\Omega_{0}=\tau z-(1-\tau)\left[p(\sigma_{0})-\beta_{0}v\right]
.
For the proof let us decompose
F
as follows

F(z\mathchar 59\relax y|\theta)=F_{0}(z\mathchar 59\relax y)+F_{1}(z\mathchar 59\relax y)_{\alpha}\theta^{\alpha}+F_{2}(z\mathchar 59\relax y)\theta_{\alpha}\theta^{\alpha}\,\mathchar 59\relax

(B.5)

where it is used that

q_{\alpha}\theta^{\alpha}k_{\beta}\theta^{\beta}=-\dfrac{1}{2}q_{\alpha}k^{\alpha}\theta_{\beta}\theta^{\beta}.

(B.6)

Then, let us check that

\int\limits_{\text{\hbox to0.0pt{\hss${\hphantom{u^{2}v^{2}\tau\sigma^{i}\beta}u^{2}v^{2}\tau\sigma^{i}\beta}$\hss}}}\quad\vartriangleright_{\lambda_{0}}F\cong\Delta_{p(\sigma_{0})\mathchar 59\relax\beta_{0}}F\qquad\text{with }\lambda_{0}(\sigma_{\zeta}\mathchar 59\relax\beta)=\prod_{i}\mathbb{D}(\sigma^{i}-\sigma_{0}^{i})\cdot\mathbb{D}(\beta-\beta_{0})

(B.7)

for each
\theta
-grading separately. Firstly,
\vartriangleright_{\lambda_{0}}F_{0}(z\mathchar 59\relax y)\cong 0
, because it does not contain the differential
d\tau
, which agrees with
\Delta_{p(\sigma_{0})\mathchar 59\relax\beta_{0}}F_{0}(z\mathchar 59\relax y)\sim\dfrac{1}{\partial\theta}F_{0}(z\mathchar 59\relax y)=0
. Further, the only
potentially non-weak term in the expression
\vartriangleright_{\lambda_{0}}F_{1}(z\mathord{\mathchar 59\relax}y)_{\alpha}\theta^{\alpha}
is that proportional to
d\tau
. Namely,

\vartriangleright_{\lambda_{0}}F_{1}(z\mathord{\mathchar 59\relax}y)_{\alpha}\theta^{\alpha}\cong d^{2}ud^{2}v\lambda_{0}(\sigma\mathchar 59\relax\beta)l(\tau)F_{1}(\Omega\mathchar 59\relax y+u)_{\alpha}d\tau(z+p(\sigma)-\beta v)^{\alpha}\exp iu_{\gamma}v^{\gamma}.

(B.8)

On the other hand,

\Delta_{p(\sigma_{0})\mathchar 59\relax\beta_{0}}\left(F_{1\alpha}\theta^{\alpha}\right)=\int\limits_{\text{\hbox to0.0pt{\hss${\hphantom{u^{2}v^{2}}u^{2}v^{2}}$\hss}}}d^{2}ud^{2}v\int\limits_{\text{\hbox to0.0pt{\hss${\hphantom{\tau}\tau}$\hss}}}\dfrac{d\tau}{\tau}l(\tau)(z+p(\sigma_{0})-\beta_{0}v)^{\alpha}\dfrac{1}{\partial\theta^{\alpha}}F_{1}(\Omega_{0}\mathchar 59\relax y+u)_{\beta}\tau\theta^{\beta}\exp iu_{\gamma}v^{\gamma}=\\
=\int\limits_{\text{\hbox to0.0pt{\hss${\hphantom{u^{2}v^{2}}u^{2}v^{2}}$\hss}}}d^{2}ud^{2}v\int\limits_{\text{\hbox to0.0pt{\hss${\hphantom{\tau}\tau}$\hss}}}d\tau l(\tau)F_{1}(\Omega_{0}\mathchar 59\relax y+u)_{\alpha}(z+p(\sigma_{0})-\beta_{0}v)^{\alpha}\exp iu_{\gamma}v^{\gamma}.

(B.9)

Thus, the correctness of (
B.7
) is checked by direct comparison. For a two-form in
\theta
this is checked analogously.
The only term in
\vartriangleright_{\lambda_{0}}F_{2}(z\mathord{\mathchar 59\relax}y)\theta_{\alpha}\theta^{\alpha}\sim F_{2}(z\mathchar 59\relax y)\mathrm{d}\Omega_{\alpha}\mathrm{d}\Omega^{\alpha}
that can be non-weak is that proportional to
2\tau d\tau(z+p(\sigma)-\beta v)_{\alpha}\theta^{\alpha}
.

This leads to the following interesting properties. Firstly, if
F\cong 0
, then
\vartriangleright_{\lambda}F\cong 0
. This is true because

\int\limits_{\text{\hbox to0.0pt{\hss${\hphantom{u^{2}v^{2}\tau\sigma^{i}\beta\{h\}}u^{2}v^{2}\tau\sigma^{i}\beta\{h\}}$\hss}}}\quad\vartriangleright_{\lambda_{0}}F\cong\int_{\{h\}}\Delta_{p(\sigma_{0})\mathchar 59\relax\beta_{0}}F=\Delta_{p(\sigma_{0})\mathchar 59\relax\beta_{0}}\left(\int_{\{h\}}F\right)=0\,\mathchar 59\relax

(B.10)

where the symbol
\{h\}
denotes all homotopy parameters in
F
as before. In particular, this can be applied to the associator (
2.11
):
\vartriangleright_{\lambda}\left[(F*G)*H-F*(G*H)\right]\cong 0
. We recall that the product
*
defined in (
2.1
) involves no actual integration. From this definition of
*
it follows that the associator itself is non-vanishing. This distinction is fundamental: the associator vanishes only in the weak sense due to the absence of integration in the star-product kernel. The more consequential manifestation for our work — and crucially extendable to symmetry properties — appears in the commutator with
\gamma
(
2.10
):

\vartriangleright_{\lambda}\left[F\mathchar 59\relax\gamma\right]_{*}\cong 0.

(B.11)

One can verify by straightforward computation that when
F
is canonical (
2.12
),
the relation (
B.11
) becomes a special case of the more general one (
2.53
). This is because the measure
\lambda
is chosen in the particular form (
B.3
). Specifically, (
B.11
) coincides with (
2.53
) if in the latter the measure for variables
\tau_{12}\mathchar 59\relax\sigma_{12}^{i}\mathchar 59\relax\beta_{12}
is chosen in the form

l(\tau_{12})\prod_{i}\mathbb{D}(\sigma_{12}^{i}-\sigma_{0}^{i})\mathbb{D}(\beta_{12}-\beta_{0})\,.

(B.12)

B.2
Fields derivation

The equations that we are to solve at each step of the perturbation procedure can be written as follows

\displaystyle 2i\mathrm{d}B_{n}=

\displaystyle\mathcal{R}_{B_{n}}+\mathcal{V}_{B_{n}}=\sum_{i=1}^{n-1}\left[S_{i}\mathchar 59\relax B_{n-i}\right]_{*}+\mathcal{V}_{B_{n}}\,\mathchar 59\relax

(B.13)

\displaystyle 2i\mathrm{d}S_{n}=

\displaystyle\mathcal{R}_{S_{n}}+\mathcal{V}_{S_{n}}=\sum_{i=1}^{n-1}S_{i}*S_{n-1}-i\eta B_{n}*\gamma+\mathcal{V}_{S_{n}}\,\mathchar 59\relax

(B.14)

\displaystyle 2i\mathrm{d}W_{n}=

\displaystyle\mathcal{R}_{W_{n}}+\mathcal{V}_{W_{n}}=\sum_{i=1}^{n-1}\left\{S_{i}\mathchar 59\relax W_{n-i}\right\}_{*}+\sum_{i=1}^{n}\mathrm{d}_{x}S_{i}-i\eta B_{n\omega}*\gamma+\mathcal{V}_{W_{n}}\,\mathchar 59\relax

(B.15)

where
\mathcal{V}_{i}
denote some weak terms while
\mathcal{R}_{i}
are the non-weak
r.h.s.
of the corresponding equations.

B.2.1
Solving for
\mathbf{S_{n}}

One may wish to reproduce the shifted homotopy scheme choosing the solution for
S_{n}
in the following form

S_{n}=\dfrac{1}{2i}\vartriangleright_{\lambda_{0}}\left(\sum_{i=1}^{n-1}S_{i}*S_{n-1}-i\eta B_{n}*\gamma\right).

(B.16)

It solves equations since (
2.8
) and (
B.7
) take place. So,

S_{n}=\dfrac{1}{2i}\Delta_{p(\sigma_{0})\mathchar 59\relax\beta_{0}}\mathcal{R}_{S_{n}}+\mathcal{V}\Rightarrow\mathrm{d}S_{n}=\mathrm{d}_{z}S_{n}+\mathrm{d}_{t}S_{n}\cong\mathrm{d}_{z}S_{n}\cong\dfrac{1}{2i}\mathrm{d}_{z}\Delta_{p(\sigma_{0})\mathchar 59\relax\beta_{0}}\mathcal{R}_{S_{n}}=\dfrac{1}{2i}\mathcal{R}_{S_{n}}\,.

(B.17)

This fact can be verified directly using the previously proven properties (
2.2
), (
B.7
).

B.2.2
Solving for
\mathbf{W_{n}}

Solution to this field can also be written in terms of the homotopy operator,

W_{n}=\dfrac{1}{2i}\vartriangleright_{\lambda_{0}}\mathcal{R}_{W_{n}}\cong\dfrac{1}{2i}\vartriangleright_{\lambda_{0}}\left(\sum_{k=0}^{n}\left\{W_{k}\mathchar 59\relax S_{n-k}\right\}_{*}-i\eta B_{n\omega}*\gamma\right).

(B.18)

The last weak equality is fair because,
just as in the shifted homotopy approach,

\vartriangleright_{\lambda_{0}}\mathrm{d}_{x}S_{k}\sim\mathrm{d}_{x}\vartriangleright_{\lambda_{0}}S_{k}\sim\mathrm{d}_{x}\vartriangleright_{\lambda_{0}}\vartriangleright_{\lambda_{0}}\mathcal{R}_{S_{k}}\cong\mathrm{d}_{x}\Delta_{p(\sigma_{0})\mathchar 59\relax\beta_{0}}\Delta_{p(\sigma_{0})\mathchar 59\relax\beta_{0}}\mathcal{R}_{S_{k}}=0

(B.19)

due to
\{\Delta_{q_{1}\mathchar 59\relax\beta_{1}}\mathchar 59\relax\Delta_{q_{2}\mathchar 59\relax\beta_{2}}\}=0
.
However, differential homotopy approach and its fundamental Ansatz allows us to control the form of this expression in the second order. First of all,
B_{2\omega}
is weak. Further, we should note that according to (
2.52
), taking into account that
S_{2}
cointans
\delta(a_{i}-(1-\beta_{i}))
,

\omega(y)*S_{2}=\mathbb{D}(\sigma_{12}^{\omega}+(1-\beta_{12}))\mathbb{D}(\sigma_{2}^{\omega}-(1-\beta_{2}))\mathbb{D}(\sigma_{1}^{\omega}+(1-\beta_{1}))S_{2}(wCC)\mathchar 59\relax

(B.20)

and, consequently, the expression
\vartriangleright_{\lambda_{0}}\omega*S_{2}
takes the form

\vartriangleright_{\lambda_{0}}\omega(y)*S_{2}=P(-1+\beta_{12}\mathchar 59\relax\sigma_{12}^{\omega}\mathchar 59\relax 0)\mathbb{D}(\sigma_{2}^{\omega}-(1-\beta_{2}))\mathbb{D}(\sigma_{1}^{\omega}+(1-\beta_{1}))S_{2}(wCC).

(B.21)

To prove this one has to use that

S_{2}=\dfrac{1}{2i}\vartriangleright_{\lambda_{0}}\mathcal{R}_{S_{2}}=d^{2}u_{12}d^{2}v_{12}\lambda_{0}(\sigma_{12}\mathchar 59\relax\beta_{12})l(\tau_{12})\mathcal{R}_{S_{2}}(\Omega_{12}\mathchar 59\relax y+u_{12}|\mathrm{d}\Omega_{12})\exp iu_{12\alpha}v_{12}^{\alpha}

(B.22)

so that

\vartriangleright_{\lambda_{0}}\left(\omega*\vartriangleright_{\lambda_{0}}\mathcal{R}_{S_{2}}\right)=\vartriangleright_{\lambda_{0}}\mathbb{D}(\sigma_{12}^{\omega}+(1-\beta_{12}))\mathbb{D}(\sigma_{2}^{\omega}-(1-\beta_{2}))\mathbb{D}(\sigma_{1}^{\omega}+(1-\beta_{1}))\\
d^{2}u_{12}d^{2}v_{12}\lambda_{0}(\sigma_{12}^{1}\mathchar 59\relax\sigma_{12}^{2}\mathchar 59\relax\beta_{12})l(\tau_{12})\mathcal{R}_{S_{2}}(\Omega_{12}\mathchar 59\relax y+u_{12}|\mathrm{d}\Omega_{12})\exp iu_{12\alpha}v_{12}^{\alpha}=\\
=d^{2}u_{\zeta}d^{2}v_{\zeta}\lambda_{0}(\sigma_{\zeta}^{1}\mathchar 59\relax\sigma_{\zeta}^{2}\mathchar 59\relax\beta_{\zeta})l(\tau_{\zeta})\mathbb{D}(\sigma_{12}^{\omega}+(1-\beta_{12}))\mathbb{D}(\sigma_{2}^{\omega}-(1-\beta_{2}))\mathbb{D}(\sigma_{1}^{\omega}+(1-\beta_{1}))\\
d^{2}u_{12}d^{2}v_{12}\lambda_{0}(\sigma_{12}^{1}\mathchar 59\relax\sigma_{12}^{2}\mathchar 59\relax\beta_{12})l(\tau_{12})\mathcal{R}_{S_{2}}(\Omega_{12}^{\prime}\mathchar 59\relax y+u_{12}+u_{\zeta}|\mathrm{d}\Omega_{12}^{\prime})\exp i\left(u_{12\alpha}v_{12}^{\alpha}+u_{\zeta\beta}v_{\zeta}^{\beta}\right)\mathchar 59\relax

(B.23)

where now
\Omega_{12}^{\prime}=\tau_{12}\zeta-(1-\tau_{12})\left[p(\sigma_{12})-\beta_{12}v_{12}\right]\mathchar 59\relax\quad\zeta=\tau_{\zeta}z-(1-\tau_{\zeta})\left[p(\sigma_{\zeta})-\beta_{\zeta}v_{\zeta}\right]
. Since
\lambda_{0}(\sigma\mathchar 59\relax\beta)=\prod_{i}\mathbb{D}(\sigma^{i}-\sigma_{0}^{i})\cdot\mathbb{D}(\beta-\beta_{0})
is composed from delta functions, the product
p(\sigma_{12})
and
p(\sigma_{\zeta})
can be replaced with
p(\sigma_{0})
keeping the expression unchanged in a weak sense. For simplicity,
\sigma_{0}^{i}
can be set to zero. Then, shifting the variables
u_{12}\rightarrow u_{12}-u_{\zeta}
we arrive at the expression

d^{2}u_{12}d^{2}v_{12}d^{2}u_{\zeta}d^{2}v_{\zeta}\mathcal{R}_{S_{2}}(\Omega_{12}^{\prime}\mathchar 59\relax y+u_{12}|\mathrm{d}\Omega_{12}^{\prime})\exp i\left(u_{12\alpha}v_{12}^{\alpha}+u_{\zeta\beta}(v_{\zeta}-v_{12})^{\beta}\right)\cong\\
\cong d^{2}u_{12}d^{2}v_{12}d^{2}v_{\zeta}\delta^{(2)}(v_{\zeta}-v_{12})\mathcal{R}_{S_{2}}(\Omega_{12}^{\prime}\mathchar 59\relax y+u_{12}|\mathrm{d}\Omega_{12}^{\prime})\exp i\left(u_{12\alpha}v_{12}^{\alpha}\right)\cong\\
\cong d^{2}u_{12}d^{2}v_{12}\mathcal{R}_{S_{2}}(\Omega_{12}^{\prime\prime}\mathchar 59\relax y+u_{12}|\mathrm{d}\Omega_{12}^{\prime\prime})\exp i\left(u_{12\alpha}v_{12}^{\alpha}\right)\mathchar 59\relax

(B.24)

where

\Omega_{12}^{\prime\prime}=\tau_{12}\left[\tau_{\zeta}z-(1-\tau_{\zeta})\left(p(\sigma_{0})-\beta_{0}v_{12}\right)\right]-(1-\tau_{12})\left[p(\sigma_{0})-p_{\omega}(1-\beta_{0})-\beta_{0}v_{12}\right]=\\
=\tau_{12}\tau_{\zeta}z-(1-\tau_{12}\tau_{\zeta})\left[p(\sigma_{12}^{\prime})+p_{\omega}\sigma_{12}^{\prime}-\beta_{0}^{\prime}v_{12}\right].

(B.25)

The new coefficients in front of
p_{i}
and
v_{12}
can be easily computed

\displaystyle{\sigma_{12}^{\prime}}^{i}=

\displaystyle\left(\dfrac{\tau_{12}(1-\tau_{\zeta})}{1-\tau_{12}\tau_{\zeta}}+\dfrac{1-\tau_{12}}{1-\tau_{12}\tau_{\zeta}}\right)\sigma_{0}^{i}=\sigma_{0}^{i}\mathchar 59\relax\quad\text{for }i=1\mathord{\mathchar 59\relax}2

(B.26)

\displaystyle{\sigma_{12}^{\prime}}^{\omega}=

\displaystyle-\dfrac{1-\tau_{12}}{1-\tau_{12}\tau_{\zeta}}(1-\beta_{0})

(B.27)

\displaystyle\beta_{12}^{\prime}=

\displaystyle\left(\dfrac{\tau_{12}(1-\tau_{\zeta})}{1-\tau_{12}\tau_{\zeta}}+\dfrac{1-\tau_{12}}{1-\tau_{12}\tau_{\zeta}}\right)\beta_{0}=\beta_{0}.

(B.28)

As a result of change of the integration variables from
\tau_{12}\mathchar 59\relax\tau_{\zeta}
to
\tau=\tau_{12}\tau_{\zeta}\mathchar 59\relax{\sigma_{12}^{\prime}}^{\omega}
, taking into account that for
0<\tau_{12}<1
and
0<\tau_{\zeta}<1

\tau_{12}\tau_{\zeta}\in(0\mathchar 59\relax 1)\mathchar 59\relax\qquad-\dfrac{1-\tau_{12}}{1-\tau_{12}\tau_{\zeta}}(1-\beta_{0})\in(-(1-\beta_{0})\mathchar 59\relax 0)\mathchar 59\relax

(B.29)

the measure transforms as follows

l(\tau_{12})l(\tau_{\zeta})\lambda_{0}(\sigma_{12}^{1}\mathchar 59\relax\sigma_{12}^{2}\mathchar 59\relax\beta_{12})\mathbb{D}(\sigma_{12}^{\omega}+(1-\beta_{12}))\mathbb{D}(\sigma_{2}^{\omega}-(1-\beta_{2}))\mathbb{D}(\sigma_{1}^{\omega}+(1-\beta_{1}))\rightarrow\\
\rightarrow l(\tau)\lambda_{0}(\sigma_{12}^{1}\mathchar 59\relax\sigma_{12}^{2}\mathchar 59\relax\beta_{12})P(-1+\beta_{12}\mathchar 59\relax\sigma_{12}^{\omega}\mathchar 59\relax 0)\mathbb{D}(\sigma_{2}^{\omega}-(1-\beta_{2}))\mathbb{D}(\sigma_{1}^{\omega}+(1-\beta_{1}))\mathchar 59\relax

(B.30)

which proves (
B.21
). Analogously it can be proven that

\vartriangleright_{\lambda_{0}}S_{2}*\omega(y)=P(-1+\beta_{12}\mathchar 59\relax\sigma_{12}^{\omega}\mathchar 59\relax 0)\mathbb{D}(\sigma_{2}^{\omega}+(1-\beta_{2}))\mathbb{D}(\sigma_{1}^{\omega}-(1-\beta_{1}))S_{2}(wCC).

(B.31)

In
[
34
]
it was shown that
W_{1}
admits the following representation via
S_{1}
:

\displaystyle W_{1}(\omega C)=\hphantom{-}

\displaystyle\dfrac{1}{2i}P(\beta-1\mathchar 59\relax\sigma^{\omega}\mathchar 59\relax\sigma^{1})S_{1}(\omega C)\mathord{\mathchar 59\relax}

(B.32)

\displaystyle W_{1}(C\omega)=-

\displaystyle\dfrac{1}{2i}P(\sigma^{1}\mathchar 59\relax\sigma^{\omega}\mathchar 59\relax 1-\beta)S_{1}(C\omega)\,\mathchar 59\relax

(B.33)

that can be used to analyze terms
\vartriangleright_{\lambda_{0}}\left\{S_{1}\mathchar 59\relax W_{1}\right\}_{*}
. For instance, for the
\omega CC
ordering only
\vartriangleright_{\lambda_{0}}\omega*S_{2}(CC)
and
\vartriangleright_{\lambda_{0}}W_{1}(\omega C)*S_{1}(C)
contributes. And
\frac{1}{2i}\vartriangleright_{\lambda_{0}}W_{1}*S_{1}
can be written as

\frac{1}{2i}\vartriangleright_{\lambda_{0}}W_{1}(\omega C)*S_{1}(C)=\frac{1}{2i}\vartriangleright_{\lambda_{0}}\dfrac{1}{2i}P(\beta_{1}-1\mathchar 59\relax\sigma_{1}^{\omega}\mathchar 59\relax\sigma_{1}^{1})S_{1}(\omega C)*S_{1}(C)\cong\\
\cong\dfrac{1}{2i}P(\beta_{1}-1\mathchar 59\relax\sigma_{1}^{\omega}\mathchar 59\relax\sigma_{1}^{1})\mathbb{D}(\sigma_{12}^{\omega})\mathbb{D}(\sigma_{2}^{\omega}-(1-\beta_{2}))\vartriangleright_{\lambda_{0}}S_{1}*S_{1}(\omega CC)\cong\\
\cong\dfrac{1}{2i}P(\beta_{1}-1\mathchar 59\relax\sigma_{1}^{\omega}\mathord{\mathchar 59\relax}0)\mathbb{D}(\sigma_{12}^{\omega})\mathbb{D}(\sigma_{2}^{\omega}-(1-\beta_{2})S_{2}(\omega CC).

(B.34)

In the last weak equality, since
S_{2}=\frac{1}{2i}\vartriangleright_{\lambda_{0}}\left(S_{1}*S_{1}-i\eta B_{2}*\gamma\right)
, it was asserted that the added expression
P(\beta_{1}-1\mathchar 59\relax\sigma_{1}^{\omega}\mathord{\mathchar 59\relax}0)\mathbb{D}(\sigma_{12}^{\omega})\mathbb{D}(\sigma_{2}^{\omega}-(1-\beta_{2})\vartriangleright_{\lambda_{0}}B_{2}*\gamma
is obviously weak because now
\Omega_{1}
contains three parameters requiring differentials, but at most two of them can be present in
\mathrm{d}\Omega_{1}^{2}
. So, a solution to
W_{2}
can be written in a concise form

W_{2}(\omega CC)=\dfrac{1}{2i}\left[P(-1+\beta_{12}\mathchar 59\relax\sigma_{12}^{\omega}\mathchar 59\relax 0)\mathbb{D}(\sigma_{2}^{\omega}-(1-\beta_{2}))\mathbb{D}(\sigma_{1}^{\omega}+(1-\beta_{1}))+\right.\\
+\left.P(\beta_{1}-1\mathchar 59\relax\sigma_{1}^{\omega}\mathchar 59\relax\sigma_{1}^{1})\mathbb{D}(\sigma_{12}^{\omega})\mathbb{D}(\sigma_{2}^{\omega}-(1-\beta_{2})\right]S_{2}(wCC)\,.

(B.35)

Analogous representations for
W_{2}
can be obtained in other orderings of
\omega
and
C
.
Unfortunately, this form is unlikely to hold true at higher orders since
when generalizing (
B.34
) to higher orders to obtain an expression proportional to
S_{n}
, we have to add not only
P(\sigma_{i}^{\omega})\vartriangleright_{\lambda_{0}}B_{n}*\gamma
, but also summands like
P(\sigma_{i}^{\omega})\vartriangleright S_{i}*S_{n-i}
, which are no longer weak. Here,
P(\sigma_{i}^{\omega})
denotes the measure for the variables
\sigma_{i}^{\omega}
(in the second order, for instance, it is
P(\beta_{1}-1\mathchar 59\relax\sigma_{1}^{\omega}\mathord{\mathchar 59\relax}0)\mathbb{D}(\sigma_{12}^{\omega})\mathbb{D}(\sigma_{2}^{\omega}-(1-\beta_{2})
).

Appendix C
Comparison of shifted and differential homotopy
\mathbf{\delta B_{2}}

To obtain a projectively compact spin-local vertex,
in
[
36
]
it was suggested to replace
B_{2sh}
(
3.22
) by
B_{2sh}^{\prime}:=B_{2sh}+\delta B_{2sh}

with

\delta B_{2sh}=\dfrac{\eta}{2}\int\limits_{0}^{1}d\tau C(\tau y)\bar{*}C((\tau-1)y)k\,.

(C.1)

On the other hand, in
[
34
]
it was shown, that
B_{2pc}
(
3.23
)
also leads to a projectively compact vertex.
Naively, there is no good reason to expect
B_{2sh}^{\prime}=B_{2pc}
, despite both of them generate the same vertex
\Upsilon(\omega\mathchar 59\relax C\mathchar 59\relax C)
. Nevertheless, as we show in this Appendix, these two fields coincide.

Since, as shown in
[
27
]
, the lowest-order corrections, including
B_{2}
,
S_{1}
and
W_{1}
, are
\beta
-independent,
without loss of generality, we set
\beta=0
throughout this section.
Let
\delta B_{2pc}:=B_{2pc}-B_{2sh}
. Here, we prove that
\delta B_{2pc}=\delta B_{2sh}
hence establishing
B_{2pc}=B_{2sh}^{\prime}
.

Let us first compute
\delta B_{2sh}
.

\delta B_{2sh}=\dfrac{\eta}{2}\int\limits_{0}^{1}d\tau C(\tau y)\bar{*}C((\tau-1)y)k=\\
=\dfrac{\eta}{2}\int d\tau d^{2}r_{1}d^{2}r_{2}\delta^{2}(r_{1}-\tau y)\delta^{2}(r_{2}-(\tau-1)y)C(r_{1})\bar{*}C(r_{2})k=\\
=\dfrac{\eta}{2}\int d\tau d^{2}r_{i}d^{2}p_{i}e^{-ip_{1\alpha}(r_{1}-\tau y)^{\alpha}}e^{-ip_{2\alpha}(r_{2}-(\tau-1)y)^{\alpha}}C(r_{1})\bar{*}C(r_{2})k=\\
=\dfrac{\eta}{2}\int d\tau d^{2}r_{i}d^{2}p_{i}e^{i\left[\tau p_{+\alpha}y^{\alpha}-p_{2\alpha}y^{\alpha}-p_{1\alpha}r_{1}^{\alpha}-p_{2\alpha}r_{2}^{\alpha}\right]}C(r_{1})\bar{*}C(r_{2})k\,\mathchar 59\relax

(C.2)

where, as usual,
p_{+\alpha}=p_{1\alpha}+p_{2\alpha}
. Now it is easy to evaluate the integral over
\tau
:

\int\limits_{0}^{1}d\tau e^{i\tau p_{+\alpha}y^{\alpha}}=\dfrac{e^{ip_{+\alpha}y^{\alpha}}-1}{ip_{+\alpha}y^{\alpha}}.

(C.3)

Therefore, denoting
\exp-i(p_{1\alpha}r_{1}^{\alpha}+p_{2\alpha}r_{2}^{\alpha})\eqqcolon\mathcal{E}^{(pr)}
,

\delta B_{2sh}=\dfrac{\eta}{2i}\int d^{2}r_{i}d^{2}p_{i}\;e^{-ip_{2\alpha}y^{\alpha}}\cdot\dfrac{e^{ip_{+\alpha}y^{\alpha}}-1}{p_{+\alpha}y^{\alpha}}\mathcal{E}^{(pr)}C(r_{1})\bar{*}C(r_{2})k.

(C.4)

Here and after, entire functions like
\frac{e^{ip_{+\alpha}y^{\alpha}}-1}{p_{+\alpha}y^{\alpha}}
are understood as the power series
\sum_{n=1}\frac{i^{n}}{n!}(p_{+\alpha}y^{\alpha})^{n-1}
.

Now, let us compute
\delta B_{2pc}
resulting from the differential homotopy method. Recall that (for
\beta=0
)

B_{2sh}=-\dfrac{\eta}{4i}\int\limits_{\text{\hbox to0.0pt{\hss${\hphantom{\tau\rho\sigma^{1}\sigma^{2}}\tau\rho\sigma^{1}\sigma^{2}}$\hss}}}l(\tau)\mathbb{D}(\sigma^{2}-\sigma^{1}-1)l(-\sigma^{1})\mathbb{D}(\rho)\mathrm{d}\Omega^{2}\mathcal{E}(\Omega)C(r_{1})\bar{*}C(r_{2})k\,\mathchar 59\relax

(C.5)

B_{2pc}=\dfrac{\eta}{4i}\int\limits_{\text{\hbox to0.0pt{\hss${\hphantom{\tau\rho\sigma^{1}\sigma^{2}}\tau\rho\sigma^{1}\sigma^{2}}$\hss}}}l(\tau)\mathbb{D}(\sigma^{1}+\rho)\mathbb{D}(\sigma^{2}-\sigma^{1}-1)\left[l(-\sigma^{1})\mathrm{d}\Omega^{2}+2i\mathbb{D}(\sigma^{2})d\tau\right]\mathcal{E}(\Omega)C(r_{1})\bar{*}C(r_{2})k\,\mathchar 59\relax

(C.6)

\mathcal{E}(\Omega)=\exp i\left[\Omega_{\alpha}(y+p_{+})^{\alpha}-p_{1\alpha}p_{2}^{\alpha}-p_{1\alpha}r_{1}^{\alpha}-p_{2\alpha}r_{2}^{\alpha}\right]\mathchar 59\relax\quad\Omega_{\alpha}=\tau z_{\alpha}-(1-\tau)[p_{1\alpha}\sigma^{1}+p_{2\alpha}\sigma^{2}+\rho(y+p_{+})_{\alpha}]\,.

(C.7)

Then

\displaystyle\delta B_{2pc}=\delta B_{2}^{[1]}+\delta B_{2}^{[2]}+\delta B_{2}^{[3]}=

\displaystyle\dfrac{\eta}{4i}\int\limits_{\text{\hbox to0.0pt{\hss${\hphantom{\tau\rho\sigma^{1}\sigma^{2}}\tau\rho\sigma^{1}\sigma^{2}}$\hss}}}l(\tau)\mathbb{D}(\sigma^{1}+\rho)\mathbb{D}(\sigma^{2}-\sigma^{1}-1)l(-\sigma^{1})\mathrm{d}\Omega^{2}\mathcal{E}(\Omega)C(r_{1})\bar{*}C(r_{2})k-

(C.8)

\displaystyle-

\displaystyle\dfrac{\eta}{4i}\int\limits_{\text{\hbox to0.0pt{\hss${\hphantom{\tau\rho\sigma^{1}\sigma^{2}}\tau\rho\sigma^{1}\sigma^{2}}$\hss}}}l(\tau)\mathbb{D}(\rho)\mathbb{D}(\sigma^{2}-\sigma^{1}-1)l(-\sigma^{1})\mathrm{d}\Omega^{2}\mathcal{E}(\Omega)C(r_{1})\bar{*}C(r_{2})k+

(C.9)

\displaystyle+

\displaystyle\dfrac{\eta}{2}\int\limits_{\text{\hbox to0.0pt{\hss${\hphantom{\tau\rho\sigma^{1}\sigma^{2}}\tau\rho\sigma^{1}\sigma^{2}}$\hss}}}l(\tau)\mathbb{D}(\sigma^{1}+\rho)\mathbb{D}(\sigma^{2}-\sigma^{1}-1)\mathbb{D}(\sigma^{2})d\tau\mathcal{E}(\Omega)C(r_{1})\bar{*}C(r_{2})k.

(C.10)

Firstly, consider the last term (
C.10
). Evaluating most of integrals we obtain

\delta B_{2}^{[3]}=\dfrac{\eta}{2}\int\limits_{\text{\hbox to0.0pt{\hss${\hphantom{\tau\rho\sigma^{1}\sigma^{2}}\tau\rho\sigma^{1}\sigma^{2}}$\hss}}}l(\tau)\mathbb{D}(\sigma^{1}+\rho)\mathbb{D}(\sigma^{2}-\sigma^{1}-1)\mathbb{D}(\sigma^{2})d\tau\mathcal{E}(\Omega)C(r_{1})\bar{*}C(r_{2})k=\\
=\dfrac{\eta}{2}\int\limits_{\text{\hbox to0.0pt{\hss${\hphantom{\tau\rho\sigma^{1}\sigma^{2}}\tau\rho\sigma^{1}\sigma^{2}}$\hss}}}l(\tau)\mathbb{D}(\sigma^{2})\mathbb{D}(\sigma^{1}+1)\mathbb{D}(\rho-1)d\tau\mathcal{E}(\Omega)C(r_{1})\bar{*}C(r_{2})k=\dfrac{\eta}{2}\int\limits_{\text{\hbox to0.0pt{\hss${\hphantom{\tau}\tau}$\hss}}}l(\tau)d\tau\mathcal{E}_{1}(\Omega)C(r_{1})\bar{*}C(r_{2})k\,\mathchar 59\relax

(C.11)

where
\mathcal{E}_{1}(\Omega)=\mathcal{E}(\Omega(\sigma^{1}=-1\mathchar 59\relax\sigma^{2}=0))
. Let us consider the exponent in more detail,

\mathcal{E}_{1}=\exp i\left[\left(\tau z+(1-\tau)p_{1}\right)_{\alpha}(y+p_{+})^{\alpha}-p_{1\alpha}p_{2}^{\alpha}-\sum p_{i\alpha}r_{i}^{\alpha}\right]=\\
=\exp i\left[\tau(z_{\alpha}y^{\alpha}+z_{\alpha}p_{+}^{\alpha}-p_{1\alpha}p_{2}^{\alpha})-(1-\tau)y_{\alpha}p_{+}^{\alpha}+(1-\tau)y_{\alpha}p_{2}^{\alpha}-\sum p_{i\alpha}r_{i}^{\alpha}\right]=\mathcal{E}_{0}\cdot e^{-i(1-\tau)y_{\alpha}p_{+}^{\alpha}}\mathchar 59\relax

(C.12)

where
\mathcal{E}_{0}=\exp i\left[\tau(z_{\alpha}y^{\alpha}+z_{\alpha}p_{+}^{\alpha}-p_{1\alpha}p_{2}^{\alpha})+(1-\tau)y_{\alpha}p_{2}^{\alpha}-\sum p_{i\alpha}r_{i}^{\alpha}\right]
.

Now compute the sum of the first two terms, (
C.8
) and (
C.9
). To this end, we make the shift
\sigma^{2}\rightarrow\sigma^{2}+\sigma^{1}+1
in the both terms,
\rho\rightarrow\rho-\sigma^{1}
in the first term, and then evaluate the integrals over all variables except for
\tau
.

In the first term (
C.8
),
\Omega_{\alpha}=\tau z_{\alpha}-(1-\tau)\left[p_{1\alpha}\sigma^{1}+p_{2\alpha}(\sigma^{1}+1)-\sigma^{1}(y+p_{+})_{\alpha}\right]=\tau z_{\alpha}-(1-\tau)(p_{2\alpha}-\sigma^{1}y_{\alpha})

\delta B_{2}^{[1]}=\dfrac{\eta}{4i}\int\limits_{\text{\hbox to0.0pt{\hss${\hphantom{\tau\sigma^{1}}\tau\sigma^{1}}$\hss}}}l(\tau)l(-\sigma^{1})2d\sigma^{1}d\tau y_{\alpha}(z+p_{2})^{\alpha}(\tau-1)\mathcal{E}C(r_{1})\bar{*}C(r_{2})k.

(C.13)

The exponent in this expression is

\mathcal{E}=\exp i\left[(\tau z-(1-\tau)(p_{2}-\sigma^{1}y))_{\alpha}(y+p_{+})^{\alpha}-p_{1\alpha}p_{2}^{\alpha}-\sum p_{i\alpha}r_{i}^{\alpha}\right]=\\
=\exp i\left[\tau(z_{\alpha}y^{\alpha}+z_{\alpha}p_{+}^{\alpha}-p_{1\alpha}p_{2}^{\alpha})+(1-\tau)y_{\alpha}p_{2}^{\alpha}+(1-\tau)\sigma^{1}y_{\alpha}p_{+}^{\alpha}-\sum p_{i\alpha}r_{i}^{\alpha}\right]=\mathcal{E}_{0}e^{i\sigma^{1}(1-\tau)y_{\alpha}p_{+}^{\alpha}}.

(C.14)

In the second term (
C.9
), the exponent has the same form, as it is
\rho
-independent. Now we are in a position to compute the preexponential factor. Since in the second term (
C.9
)
\Omega=\tau z-(1-\tau)\left[p_{2}+\sigma^{1}p_{+}\right]
we find

\delta B_{2}^{[2]}=\dfrac{\eta}{4i}\int\limits_{\text{\hbox to0.0pt{\hss${\hphantom{\tau\sigma^{1}}\tau\sigma^{1}}$\hss}}}l(\tau)l(-\sigma^{1})2d\sigma^{1}d\tau p_{+\alpha}(z+p_{2})^{\alpha}(\tau-1)\mathcal{E}C(r_{1})\bar{*}C(r_{2})k\,.

(C.15)

Thus,

\delta B_{2}^{[1]}+\delta B_{2}^{[2]}=\dfrac{\eta}{2i}\int\limits_{\text{\hbox to0.0pt{\hss${\hphantom{\tau\sigma^{1}}\tau\sigma^{1}}$\hss}}}d\sigma_{1}d\tau l(\tau)l(-\sigma^{1})e^{i\sigma^{1}(1-\tau)y_{\alpha}p_{+}^{\alpha}}(\tau-1)(y+p_{+})_{\alpha}(z+p_{2})^{\alpha}\mathcal{E}_{0}C(r_{1})\bar{*}C(r_{2})k=\\
=\dfrac{\eta}{2}\int\limits_{\text{\hbox to0.0pt{\hss${\hphantom{\tau}\tau}$\hss}}}d\tau l(\tau)\dfrac{1-e^{-i(1-\tau)y_{\alpha}p_{+}^{\alpha}}}{y_{\beta}p_{+}^{\beta}}(y+p_{+})_{\alpha}(z+p_{2})^{\alpha}\mathcal{E}_{0}C(r_{1})\bar{*}C(r_{2})k\,.

(C.16)

Since

y_{\alpha}p_{+}^{\alpha}-(y+p_{+})_{\alpha}(z+p_{2})^{\alpha}=y_{\alpha}p_{1}^{\alpha}-y_{\alpha}z^{\alpha}-p_{+\alpha}z^{\alpha}+p_{2\alpha}p_{1}^{\alpha}=(y+p_{+})_{\alpha}p_{1}^{\alpha}-(y+p_{+})_{\alpha}z^{\alpha}=(y+p_{+})_{\alpha}(p_{1}-z)^{\alpha}\,\mathord{\mathchar 59\relax}

the total correction is

\delta B_{2pc}=\dfrac{\eta}{2}\int_{0}^{1}d\tau\dfrac{(y+p_{+})_{\alpha}(z+p_{2})^{\alpha}+\left[y_{\alpha}p_{+}^{\alpha}-(y+p_{+})_{\alpha}(z+p_{2})^{\alpha}e^{-i(1-\tau)y_{\alpha}p_{+}^{\alpha}}\right]}{y_{\beta}p_{+}^{\beta}}\mathcal{E}_{0}C(r_{1})\bar{*}C(r_{2})k=\\
=\dfrac{\eta}{2}\int_{0}^{1}d\tau\dfrac{(y+p_{+})_{\alpha}(z+p_{2})^{\alpha}+(y+p_{+})_{\alpha}(p_{1}-z)^{\alpha}e^{-i(1-\tau)y_{\alpha}p_{+}^{\alpha}}}{y_{\beta}p_{+}^{\beta}}\mathcal{E}_{0}C(r_{1})\bar{*}C(r_{2})k.

(C.17)

Let us evaluate the integral

\int_{0}^{1}d\tau\mathcal{E}_{0}=\int_{0}^{1}d\tau\exp i\left[\tau(z_{\alpha}y^{\alpha}+z_{\alpha}p_{+}^{\alpha}-p_{1\alpha}p_{2}^{\alpha})+(1-\tau)y_{\alpha}p_{2}^{\alpha}\right]\mathcal{E}^{(pr)}=\\
=\dfrac{1}{i(z_{\alpha}y^{\alpha}+z_{\alpha}p_{+}^{\alpha}-p_{1\alpha}p_{2}^{\alpha}-y_{\alpha}p_{2}^{\alpha})}\cdot\left(e^{i(z_{\alpha}y^{\alpha}+z_{\alpha}p_{+}^{\alpha}-p_{1\alpha}p_{2}^{\alpha})}-e^{iy_{\alpha}p_{2}^{\alpha}}\right)\mathcal{E}^{(pr)}=\\
=\dfrac{1}{i(z+p_{2})_{\alpha}(y+p_{+})^{\alpha}}\cdot\left(e^{i(z_{\alpha}y^{\alpha}+z_{\alpha}p_{+}^{\alpha}-p_{1\alpha}p_{2}^{\alpha})}-e^{iy_{\alpha}p_{2}^{\alpha}}\right)\mathcal{E}^{(pr)}.

(C.18)

Analogous calculation yields

\int_{0}^{1}d\tau\mathcal{E}_{0}e^{-i(1-\tau)y_{\alpha}p_{+}^{\alpha}}=\int_{0}^{1}d\tau\exp i\left[\tau(z_{\alpha}y^{\alpha}+z_{\alpha}p_{+}^{\alpha}-p_{1\alpha}p_{2}^{\alpha})-(1-\tau)y_{\alpha}p_{1}^{\alpha}\right]\mathcal{E}^{(pr)}=\\
=\dfrac{1}{i(z_{\alpha}y^{\alpha}+z_{\alpha}p_{+}^{\alpha}-p_{1\alpha}p_{2}^{\alpha}+y_{\alpha}p_{1}^{\alpha})}\cdot\left(e^{i(z_{\alpha}y^{\alpha}+z_{\alpha}p_{+}^{\alpha}-p_{1\alpha}p_{2}^{\alpha})}-e^{-iy_{\alpha}p_{1}^{\alpha}}\right)\mathcal{E}^{(pr)}=\\
=\dfrac{1}{i(z-p_{1})_{\alpha}(y+p_{+})^{\alpha}}\cdot\left(e^{i(z_{\alpha}y^{\alpha}+z_{\alpha}p_{+}^{\alpha}-p_{1\alpha}p_{2}^{\alpha})}-e^{-iy_{\alpha}p_{1}^{\alpha}}\right)\mathcal{E}^{(pr)}.

(C.19)

Thus,

\delta B_{2pc}=\dfrac{\eta}{2i}\left[e^{iy_{\alpha}p_{2}^{\alpha}}-e^{i(z_{\alpha}y^{\alpha}+z_{\alpha}p_{+}^{\alpha}-p_{1\alpha}p_{2}^{\alpha})}+e^{i(z_{\alpha}y^{\alpha}+z_{\alpha}p_{+}^{\alpha}-p_{1\alpha}p_{2}^{\alpha})}-e^{-iy_{\alpha}p_{1}^{\alpha}}\right]\dfrac{1}{y_{\alpha}p_{+}^{\alpha}}\mathcal{E}^{(pr)}C(r_{1})\bar{*}C(r_{2})k=\\
=\dfrac{\eta}{2i}\cdot\dfrac{e^{ip_{1\alpha}y^{\alpha}}-e^{-ip_{2\alpha}y^{\alpha}}}{p_{+\alpha}y^{\alpha}}\mathcal{E}^{(pr)}C(r_{1})\bar{*}C(r_{2})k=\dfrac{\eta}{2i}e^{-ip_{2\alpha}y^{\alpha}}\dfrac{e^{ip_{+\alpha}y^{\alpha}}-1}{p_{+\alpha}y^{\alpha}}\mathcal{E}^{(pr)}C(r_{1})\bar{*}C(r_{2})k=\delta B_{2sh}.

(C.20)

\Box

-computations

\mathrm{d}
on (

) (the factors resulting from the action of
\mathrm{d}
are highlighted while the weak terms are discarded)

2i\mathrm{d}W_{2}^{(2)}|_{CC\omega\omega}\cong-\frac{\eta^{2}}{16}\int\limits_{\text{\hbox to0.0pt{\hss${\hphantom{\tau_{1}\rho_{1}\beta_{1}\sigma_{1}^{1}\sigma_{1}^{2}\sigma_{1}^{\omega 1}\sigma_{1}^{\omega 2}\tau_{2}\rho_{2}\beta_{2}\sigma_{2}^{1}\sigma_{2}^{2}\sigma_{2}^{\omega 1}\sigma_{2}^{\omega 2}\tau_{12}\sigma_{12}^{1}\sigma_{12}^{2}\sigma_{12}^{\omega 1}\sigma_{12}^{\omega 2}a_{1}a_{2}\beta_{12}}\tau_{1}\rho_{1}\beta_{1}\sigma_{1}^{1}\sigma_{1}^{2}\sigma_{1}^{\omega 1}\sigma_{1}^{\omega 2}\tau_{2}\rho_{2}\beta_{2}\sigma_{2}^{1}\sigma_{2}^{2}\sigma_{2}^{\omega 1}\sigma_{2}^{\omega 2}\tau_{12}\sigma_{12}^{1}\sigma_{12}^{2}\sigma_{12}^{\omega 1}\sigma_{12}^{\omega 2}a_{1}a_{2}\beta_{12}}$\hss}}}\mathrm{d}\Omega_{2}^{2}C(r_{1}\mathchar 59\relax\bar{y}\mathchar 59\relax K)\bar{*}C(r_{2}\mathchar 59\relax\bar{y}\mathchar 59\relax K)\bar{*}\omega(r_{\omega 1}\mathchar 59\relax\bar{y}\mathchar 59\relax K)\bar{*}\omega(r_{\omega 2}\mathchar 59\relax\bar{y}\mathchar 59\relax K)\big|_{r_{i}=0}\times\\
\times\mu^{\prime}(\beta_{12})\mu(\rho_{2}\mathchar 59\relax\beta_{2})l(\tau_{12})P(-\overrightarrow{\sigma_{12}}\mathchar 59\relax\sigma_{12}^{\omega 2}\mathchar 59\relax\sigma_{12}^{\omega 1}\mathchar 59\relax 0)\mathbb{D}(\sigma_{12}^{1})\mathbb{D}(\sigma_{12}^{2})\times\\

(D.1)

(D.6)

+\bm{\mathbb{D}(a_{1})}l(\tau_{1})\mathbb{D}(\overrightarrow{\sigma_{2}}-a_{2})\mathbb{D}(\tau_{2})\mathbb{D}(\sigma_{1}^{1})\mathbb{D}(\sigma_{1}^{2}-\overrightarrow{\sigma_{1}})\mathbb{D}(\sigma_{2}^{1}-\overrightarrow{\sigma_{2}})\mathbb{D}(\sigma_{2}^{2})\times\\
\times\mathbb{D}(\sigma_{1}^{\omega 1}-a_{1})\mathbb{D}(\sigma_{1}^{\omega 2}-a_{1})P(-\overrightarrow{\sigma_{2}}\mathord{\mathchar 59\relax}\sigma_{2}^{\omega 2}\mathchar 59\relax\sigma_{2}^{\omega 1}\mathord{\mathchar 59\relax}\sigma_{2}^{2})\mathbb{D}(\sigma_{12}^{\omega 1})\mathbb{D}(\sigma_{12}^{\omega 2}){\mu}(\beta_{1})\mathrm{d}{\Omega_{1}}^{2}\mathcal{E}(\Omega_{1}\mathchar 59\relax\Omega_{2})

and

(E.19)

) can be taken simply at

.

) with the opposite sign.

(E.20)

where
